The reading of "the final PDF still shows B's text" in the pixel half is this lane's: B's words look as they do without the attack, not that no text-layer box overlaps them. ADR 0346, item 7, is closed by this: the cause was not the line-height rules.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-15): criterion 6 is ticked. "Clean" is read as above, at level 1 of `\tracingcommands`: the unexpandable commands TeX executes, not the expansion of macros. The run is one fixed seed, so it is the same batch on every run; a failure would name the equation by its paragraph. Criterion 5 stays unticked and was not worked on: its pixel half fails because the class lets a tall inline equation paint outside its line (see the earlier note and ADR 0346, item 7), and the fix is in the class's line boxes (`packages/export-latex/class`), which is the export lane's.

**Notes:** Lane security-2 (2026-11-13): criteria 2, 3 and 4 are ticked (see Evidence). Criterion 1 stays unticked: the corpus is refused by the scan and by the gate the converter calls (`blocks.ts`), but the commit-time run through the room with the injected `mathGate` is not exercised in `tests/security`. Lane SEC-027-039 (2026-11-13, round 2): `tests/security/test/math-commit-gate.test.ts` (35 tests, passing) now runs the corpus of definitions and primitives through a room with the real gate injected (each refused as invalid_op with I8, nothing stored, an ordinary equation accepted) and through the converter (a placeholder and a `math_refused` diagnostic, the string absent from every bundle file). Criterion 1 (lane U-SEC-033-048): the room host now injects the gate, and the production path refuses the corpus (see Evidence); the commit-time half was previously unproven for real commits. Round 2 fix (2026-11-13): the gate is injected in `room-host.ts` and criterion 1 is ticked (see Evidence). A formula a document already held is not re-checked (`previous` in the validator), so content stored before this change is not refused retroactively; it still meets the converter's gate. Criterion 5 needs a converter run and the sandbox under Podman. Criterion 6 needs the compile of each accepted input in the sandbox and its token trace; the properties here check only the gate's decisions. Podman was not used. Batch of 2026-11-13 (export worker): nothing ticked there. The equation gate (`latex` attribute, I8 at commit time) is in `packages/export-latex` and the control plane; the end-to-end case needs a run of the converter and the sandbox under Podman. Each fuzz target depends on the emitters of SEC-033. Lane V-SEC-033-048 (2026-11-13, round 4): criterion 5 stays unticked. `services/export-worker/src/sec-034-region-equation.e2e.test.ts` (Podman, the worker image `docedit-export-worker:test`, the real converter and sandbox; 2 passed, 1 expected failure) puts the strongest accepted equation into region A (`\hspace{2em}` and `\mkern36mu` at their bounds, a `\bigg` pair around a fraction nested six levels with radicals, and `\text`). The extracted text keeps region B's price sentence and the equation text. On the rendered page (`pdftoppm`, 72 dpi) the equation's glyphs overlap headings and paragraphs of regions A and B (for example "second" in "The second region"), so the pixel half fails; it is recorded as `it.fails` and turns red when the gap closes. Cause not confirmed: `docedit.lua` sets each line's height from `tallest_run` and `docedit.line_height` (the `exact` and `multiple` rules), and the overflow is not explained by that path alone; a check of the line boxes in the class is needed (ADR 0346, item 7). Criterion 6 stays unticked: no fuzz target compiles accepted inputs in the sandbox; a fixed set of three compiles is not a fuzz run and is not claimed here.

### SEC-035 Prove the compile sandbox contains a compromised compile

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | L | must | Operator |

**Spec:** 10 §3.4, §5 (adversarial sandbox tests); 09 §9.3 · **Requirements:** R5, R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, BE-005, EXP-068, EXP-070, EXP-015, EXP-073

As an operator, I want a suite that runs hostile TeX and Lua inside the real sandbox, so that I know a fully compromised compile still gets nothing.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a test bundle that, from inside a compile, tries to read files outside its working directory (host files, other jobs' directories, environment variables, service credentials), write outside its temporary directory, open sockets (DNS and TCP, including LuaTeX sockets), and run commands (shell escape, `os.execute`, `io.popen`), when it compiles, then every attempt fails.
- [x] Given a bundle that exhausts memory beyond 1.5 GiB, loops beyond 120 seconds, forks repeatedly, or produces more than 2,000 pages, 200 MiB of output or 256 MiB of working files, when it compiles, then the job is killed or fails at the limit and the host is unaffected.
- [x] Given a job ran and ended, when the next job starts on the same host, then nothing the first wrote is visible, because each sandbox is single-use.
- [x] Given the image, when checked, then LuaTeX is version 1.17 or later, `openin_any` and `openout_any` are `paranoid`, and `--safer` is not used.
- [x] Given the worker's environment, when inspected, then it holds no credentials other than a write-only upload location for the one job.
- [ ] Given the suite, when CI runs, then it runs on every change to the image, the class or the worker, and a release is blocked by any failure.

**Evidence:** services/export-worker/src/sandbox.escape.test.ts (lane U-SEC-033-048, 2026-11-13; 23 of 23 passed, run with Vitest in Node.js against the real image `docedit-export-worker:test` in Podman through the machine route with cgroupfs, LuaTeX 1.24.0; the run took 652 s). Criteria 1 to 4: the attempts to read outside the work directory, write, open sockets, run commands, exhaust memory, fork, exceed pages, output and working files, and the single-use and LuaTeX settings are each tested there. Criterion 5: services/export-worker/src/sec-035-sandbox-env.test.ts (2 of 2 passed in Podman) runs `env` inside a job: no credential-like variable name, and no value holding key material or a URL with a password. Criterion 6 (CI) stays open: the workflow is a root configuration.

**Notes:** Lane w8 (2026-11-17): blocked, nothing new ticked. What exists: the job `export` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` builds the worker image and runs `services/export-worker` (including sandbox.escape.test.ts and sec-035-sandbox-env.test.ts) with `DOCEDIT_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`, so a missing sandbox fails instead of skipping; ci.yml runs on every pull request, which covers every change to the image, the class and the worker. What is missing: no hosted run of that job has been seen (the repository's remote is `origin` on GitHub per the owner decisions of 2026-11-15, but nothing is pushed from a lane), and `.github/workflows/release.yml` does not require the ci jobs to have passed for the tagged commit (its `needs` chain holds only its own jobs), so "a release is blocked by any failure" is not shown. Adding that gate edits `.github/`, which is root configuration; the integrator should add it (for example a `needs` on a check of the ci conclusion for the tag's commit, or a required status check on the release environment) and then show a green run. Criterion 6 stays unticked.

**Notes:** Lane w8 (2026-11-26, round 6): blocked, nothing new ticked. No decision of docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md covers this gate. The release gate is still root configuration (`.github/`) that a lane may not edit, and no hosted run exists here. The integrator can add the gate; this criterion then needs its green run.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-15): blocked, nothing new ticked. Criterion 6 is the only one open. It needs a hosted CI job that runs the suite on every change to the image, the class or the worker, and a release gate that a failure blocks. Neither can be shown from this checkout: the workflow files are root configuration (`.github/`), which a lane does not edit, and the suite needs a runner with Podman and the worker image. The suite itself was not re-run for this story; services/export-worker/src/sec-033-unescaped.escape.test.ts (SEC-033 criterion 5) ran in the same sandbox on 2026-11-15 and passed.

**Notes:** Phase 1a: the demo already compiles user content in a container without network. Cross-check: moved from phase 1a to 1b, because the sandbox it attacks (EXP-068, EXP-070, EXP-073) is built in 1b; phase 0 spike S4 already runs first escape tests. Batch of 2026-11-13 (export worker): nothing ticked. The static part of criterion 4 (the image's configuration as written) is in `services/export-worker/src/engine-config.test.ts`; the criterion itself is about the built image, which needs Podman. The adversarial attempts of criteria 1 to 3 are in `services/export-worker/src/sandbox.escape.test.ts`, which was not run here (no Podman). Criterion 5 stays open: the static search in `services/export-worker/src/image-secrets.test.ts` finds no credential in the worker's environment, but the criterion is checked on the running container (`services/export-worker/src/image-secrets.test.ts`), but the criterion is checked on the running container.

### SEC-036 Prove worker outputs are treated as hostile

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Viewer |

**Spec:** 10 §3.1 (hostile PDF), §3.4 (outputs hostile); 09 §9.2, §9.3 (7) · **Requirements:** R5, R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, FE-071, EXP-055, EXP-064

As a viewer, I want a malicious PDF, log or layout file produced by a compromised worker to be unable to reach my session, so that opening a preview is safe.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a PDF with embedded JavaScript, launch and URI actions and form scripts, when it is shown in the viewer, then nothing runs, and the frame is cross-origin and sandboxed without same-origin access.
- [x] Given code running inside the viewer frame, when it tries to read the host DOM, the token or storage, then it cannot.
- [x] Given a `layout.json` that is invalid, oversized or has extra members, when it is read, then it is rejected against its schema, the job reports a diagnostic, and nothing uses it.
- [x] Given user text that imitates log lines (fake errors, file and line markers), when the log is parsed, then no diagnostic is created for another block, the parser is bounded, and raw logs are shown only to callers with `edit`.
- [x] Given a downloadable `tex` bundle, when it is produced, then it carries the notice that it should be compiled with the same restrictions.
- [x] Given result files, when they are served, then they come from the asset origin with fixed content types and `nosniff`.

**Evidence:** Criterion 3 (`services/export-worker`): `src/layout.ts` (`parseRawLayout`) refuses a report with a member the class does not write, at the top or in a page, a page size that is not an integer, data over `maxBytes` and more pages than `maxPages`. `src/layout.test.ts` ("refuses a report with extra members, oversized data or too many pages") tests each. `src/job.ts` drops a report that fails the parser or `validateLayout`, adds the `layout_invalid` diagnostic and stores no `layout.json`; `src/job.test.ts` ("never uses a hostile layout report") checks that for four hostile reports. Criterion 4: `src/diagnostics.test.ts` shows that imitated log lines create no entry for another block and that the parser is bounded; the last clause (raw logs only to callers with `edit`) is the API's, EXP-066, and is not in this directory, so the criterion stays open. Criterion 6 (lane security-2, 2026-11-13): tests/security/test/tex-bundle-notice.test.ts builds the archive with `texArchive` (`packages/export-latex/src/archive.ts`), reads the tar back and checks that its README holds the same-restrictions notice and the settings of 09 §9.3, for every seq and chain head, and that the bundle files travel beside it. Criteria 1, 2 and 5 are the viewer's and the asset origin's and are not ticked here. Podman was not used. Criterion 4 (lane SEC-027-039, 2026-11-13, round 3): tests/security/test/sec-036-raw-log.route.test.ts (memory, the real service with the export route; `POST /v1/documents/{id}/exports`, the job claimed and completed on the queue with a hostile log written under the job prefix). A requester with `edit` gets `result.log` (a signed name); the same requester with only `view` on the document gets no `log` in `result` and no text of the hostile log. The two other clauses (no diagnostic for another block, a bounded parser) are in services/export-worker/src/diagnostics.test.ts ("maps file and line errors to the block of that line", "never creates an entry from the text of a box dump", "stops at its bounds with a truncation entry", and the fuzz "never throws, never passes its bounds"). Criteria 1 and 2 (lane w8, 2026-11-17): packages/element/e2e/pdf-viewer.spec.ts ("a PDF with scripts, launch actions and form scripts runs nothing; the host is unaffected") with the hostile PDF of packages/element/demo/pdf-hostile.ts, which now also holds a URI link action (clicked by the test) beside the OpenAction, page and form scripts and the launch actions. Run in real Chromium, Firefox and WebKit on this Windows machine (`pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts pdf-viewer --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit`, port 4848): the whole spec 26 passed, 1 skipped by design (the signed-URL test in Firefox, where PDF responses are filtered on this machine), before the URI link was added; the scripts test passed again in the three browsers after it. In each: no request reaches the demo server's `/pwned` endpoint, no dialog or popup, the host's title and address are unchanged, and the frame is on another origin with `sandbox="allow-scripts allow-same-origin"` (the frame's own origin, not the host's), or opaque with `allow-scripts` when served from the host's origin. From inside the frame, the host DOM (`window.parent.document`) is blocked, the host cookie is not visible and the host's localStorage is not the frame's. The viewer draws on a canvas with no annotation layer and no scripting (src/pdf/viewer/main.ts), and the host passes it only the PDF and strings (src/pdf/messages.ts), never the token; FE-071 holds the rest of that evidence.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-18, criterion 6):** tests/security/test/sec-036-result-origin.test.ts, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node tests/security/test/sec-036-result-origin.test.ts` (4 tests passed; without the change to `exportJobOf` three of them fail). The real service runs in process (memory storage) with `assetOrigin` and `assetUrlKey` set, over the real asset origin handler on an ephemeral port. A job made through `POST /v1/documents/{id}/exports` and completed on the queue gives `url`, `layout` and `log` that name the asset origin under `/e/{tenant}/{job}/{name}` with `exp`, `kid` and `sig`, and nothing of the object store. Fetched over HTTP, each file is served with the fixed content type of its extension (`application/pdf`, `application/json`, `text/plain; charset=utf-8`) although the worker stored it as `text/html`, with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox`, `Content-Disposition: attachment`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`; a hostile log with markup arrives as text. The origin refuses (403, or 404 for a name with no served kind, 405 for a method that is not GET or HEAD) another file under the same signature, another job, another tenant, a changed signature, an extra query member, no query, a path-traversal name and an expired URL; an asset URL is not accepted as a result URL and the other way round; `signExportUrl` throws for a name with no listed kind. Built: services/asset-worker/src/signing.ts (`signExportUrl`, `verifyExportUrl`, `EXPORT_SERVED`, `exportKindOf`, a shared query check), origin.ts (the `/e/` branch), keys.ts (`exportFile`); services/document-service `Kit.exportUrl` (control-plane/kit.ts), used by `exportJobOf` in export-routes.ts and set in server.ts when `DOCEDIT_ASSET_ORIGIN` and `DOCEDIT_ASSET_URL_KEY` are set. Spec: docs/spec/09-export-latex.md (the job result) and 10-security.md (the content-type row). Also run: services/asset-worker (all), packages/tck and packages/protocol/test/prose.test.ts, passed.

**Notes:** Lane w8 (2026-11-18): criterion 6 ticked on owner decision 66 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md: the asset origin, with the headers we control, not presigned store URLs); the two older notes at the end of this story say it was open and are stale. Decisions made here, not in the spec: results are served as downloads (`attachment`) with `private, no-store`, because a job's files are not content-addressed and the URL names no hash, so the origin cannot check a hash as it does for assets; the origin answers `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` for results, because the element reads `layout.json` and the viewer reads the PDF with a cross-origin `fetch`, and the URL is a signed capability without credentials; the listed kinds are pdf, json, log, txt, tar, html and md (the files the export worker writes), and any other name is never signed. Without an asset origin set (development and tests), the route still returns the object store's URL, as before; a deployment that serves results must set the origin. Not changed: the sealed final PDF of a workflow (`GET /documents/{id}/workflow/final`, FRW-049) is a separate route and was not looked at here, and a host that frames a result in an `<iframe>` would meet the `sandbox` and `attachment` headers (the product's own viewer reads the bytes). Criteria 1 and 2 are not looked at again. Earlier note: Lane w8 (2026-11-17): criteria 1 and 2 are read as "no access to the host's origin": the frame has its own origin, which `allow-same-origin` keeps so that PDF.js's worker and the frame's own storage work (ADR 0039 §9); it never shares the host's. If the owner means a sandbox without `allow-same-origin` always, the viewer's design would need to change; say so and these two ticks are to be reopened. Criterion 6 stays open as before: results are served by presigned object-store URLs, not from the asset origin, and which origin serves them is an open decision (ADR 0256 item 10), not among the owner decisions of 2026-11-15.

**Notes:** Lane SEC-027-039 (2026-11-13, round 4): criterion 5 stays unticked. Export results are returned as presigned object-store URLs (`services/document-service/src/objects/s3.ts`, `signedUrl`, called from `services/document-service/src/control-plane/export-routes.ts`). The presigned URL sets no response content type and no `nosniff`, and the asset origin (`services/asset-worker/src/origin.ts`) does not serve results. Which origin serves results is an open decision (ADR 0256, item 10). Criteria 1 and 2 need a browser run of the viewer frame, not in this directory.

**Notes:** Batch of 2026-11-13 (export worker). Criterion 4 needs the raw-log route of EXP-066 with its `edit` check (ticked in round 3, see Evidence). Lane SEC-027-039 (2026-11-13, round 2): the route is in `services/document-service/src/control-plane/export-routes.ts`; no route test in `tests/security` yet, so the criterion stays unticked (ADR 0256, item 6). Lane U-SEC-033-048: the route test is now tests/security/test/sec-036-raw-log-route.test.ts (Node.js, memory storage, 2 of 2 passed): the requester with `edit` gets the signed log URL, and after a demotion to `view` gets no `log`.

## Asset and import workers

### SEC-037 Prove uploads cannot claim or overwrite assets

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Owner |

**Spec:** 10 §3.5 (overwriting or claiming an asset, referencing an asset one never uploaded); 06 §4.4; 03 §8 (I4) · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, BE-042, BE-043, BE-044, BE-045

As an owner, I want nobody able to read another document's image by naming it, so that attachments are private to the documents they belong to.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given an upload that declares the hash of an existing asset, when it is processed, then the declared hash is ignored and the final object is keyed by the worker's own hash.
- [x] Given the same bytes uploaded by two tenants, when both are processed, then each tenant has a separate object, and one tenant cannot overwrite the other's.
- [x] Given a commit that references an asset the submitter never uploaded for this document, an asset of another document, or an asset of another tenant, when it is submitted, then it is rejected with `invalid_op`.
- [x] Given a caller with `view` on document B, when they resolve an asset attached only to document A, then no URL is returned.
- [x] Given a quarantined or failed asset, when a URL is requested for it, then none is issued.
- [x] Given guessed asset identifiers, when they are requested, then nothing is revealed.

**Evidence:** services/asset-worker. Criterion 1: test/worker.test.ts ('ignores a declared hash of an existing asset, of this tenant or another, and overwrites nothing', and 'is re-encoded, hashed by the worker ...'), test/s3.test.ts ('writes the re-encoded file under its own hash ...'). Criterion 2: test/worker.test.ts ('give each tenant its own object under its own prefix', 'never overwrites an existing object'), test/s3.test.ts ('gives two tenants who upload the same bytes one object each ...'). Criteria 4 to 6 (lane U-SEC-033-048, 2026-11-13): services/document-service/test/control-plane/sec-037-asset-refusals.test.ts (6 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman, all pass; `pnpm exec vitest run` in services/document-service): a quarantined asset and a failed asset attached to the content get no URL while the ready one does; a viewer of B with no grant on A gets no URL for A's asset, from B or from A (403); a guessed identifier answers as the real identifier does, with the same status and body.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-18, criterion 3):** tests/security/test/sec-037-asset-refs-wire.test.ts, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node tests/security/test/sec-037-asset-refs-wire.test.ts` (4 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman, all passed). The real service over a real WebSocket: a caller who owns two documents of tenant A opens one and submits a commit that inserts a figure. Each of five references is answered `sync.reject` with `invalid_op` (the message names invariant I4 and the asset), nothing is stored (the head does not move) and the asset identifier appears nowhere in the content: an identifier that names no asset; an asset uploaded for another document of the same tenant (declared through `POST /documents/{id}/assets` by the same caller); an asset of another tenant uploaded for that tenant's document; an asset of another tenant named for this document's identifier; an asset another user of this document uploaded (declared through the route with that user's token). The control is accepted: the same figure for an asset the caller declared for this document gives `sync.ack` at the next seq and the content names it, and an asset of the other document is still refused afterwards. The source of the attached set is BE-044's (lane w5): `RegistryAssetAttachments` in services/document-service/src/data-plane/asset-attachments.ts, composed in server.ts, with its own tests in services/document-service/test/data-plane/be-044-asset-attachments.test.ts. The room half stands in tests/security/test/sec-037-asset-refs-room.test.ts.

**Notes:** Lane w8 (2026-11-18): criterion 3 ticked; all six criteria are met and the notes below that say it is blocked or open are stale. The data-plane question of docs/adr/0346-lane-V-SEC-033-048.md item 2 (the source of the set) is answered by BE-044 as built by lane w5: the set is the document's `doc_assets` rows, what its content names, and the ready assets the submitting user uploaded for this document, read from the tenant's registry before the commit. Limit carried over from BE-044's Notes (not changed here): an asset the submitter uploaded stays known to the room, so another editor of the same document could reference it before the first commit that does so is stored; across documents and tenants it is never possible, which is what this criterion asks. The refusal of the content route (`PUT /documents/{id}/content`) is `validation_failed`, as for any snapshot; the criterion is about a submitted commit. Earlier note: Lane w8 (2026-11-17): blocked, nothing new ticked; criterion 3 is the only one open and the state is as lane V left it. Checked again: `attachedAssets` is still given to the room only by `services/document-service/src/control-plane/handlers.ts` (an empty set for a new room and the attached set of the content route); `room-host.ts` passes none, so a WebSocket commit naming another document's or tenant's asset is not checked, and BE-044 (the `doc_assets` write and the `invalid_op` refusal in the persist path) is still unbuilt. Building it here means choosing the source of the set (open-time load of `readyFor(tenant, doc)` plus the content's references, against a refresh on each asset-ready event, against an async check before `RoomHost.submit`, which is synchronous and keeps commit order for a channel), a data-plane choice that docs/adr/0346-lane-V-SEC-033-048.md item 2 leaves to the owner or the main session and that the owner decisions of 2026-11-15 do not settle. It also edits `room-host.ts` (2,275 lines, the SYN and BE lanes' code), so it is left to the lane that builds BE-044; this story's test then needs a WebSocket commit for each of the three cases (unattached, other document, other tenant) next to tests/security/test/sec-037-asset-refs-room.test.ts.

**Notes:** Left open for the control plane (2026-11-06): the routes, the persist-time check (BE-044) and the resolve route (PRO-061, PRO-062) are not built. Lane SEC-027-039 (2026-11-13): no asset-reference check is found in `packages/authority/src` (search for an asset identifier in commits finds none), so criteria 3 to 6 stay unticked. The worker's side of criteria 1 and 2 holds and is tested in services/asset-worker: a hash in the job payload is ignored and the object is keyed by the worker's own hash of its output (test/worker.test.ts "a valid image", test/s3.test.ts), and the same bytes from two tenants give one object under each tenant's prefix, with a second write of an existing key skipped (test/worker.test.ts "the same bytes from two tenants", test/s3.test.ts "two tenants"). Lane U-SEC-033-048 (2026-11-13): criterion 3 stays unticked. The content route (`PUT /documents/{id}/content`, services/document-service/src/control-plane/handlers.ts) checks attached assets, but refuses with `validation_failed`, not `invalid_op`. The commit path is not shown to check: the room takes an `attachedAssets` set only when one is given (packages/authority/src/room/room.ts), and the control plane gives a new room an empty set (handlers.ts, `attachedAssets: new Set()`). No test of a WebSocket commit that names another document's asset has been run. Wiring the set into the room is a data-plane change (BE-044) not made in this lane (see docs/adr/0346-lane-U-SEC-033-048.md). Lane SEC-027-039 (2026-11-13, round 5): the asset check is in the model (packages/model/src/validate/validate.ts, `asset_not_attached`), applied by the room when it holds the set. tests/security/test/sec-037-asset-refs-room.test.ts (Node.js, 2 of 2 pass) proves the room half: an attached asset is accepted, and an unattached one is refused `invalid_op` with the document unchanged. Criterion 3 stays unticked: the data plane does not fill the set per document, and the other-document and other-tenant cases are not run (ADR 0256, item 16). Lane V-SEC-033-048 (2026-11-13): not closed. The room validates a WebSocket commit against a static `attachedAssets` set given at construction (packages/authority/src/room/room.ts), and room-host.ts passes none; a live source (the ready assets of the document plus the references of its current content, read before each commit) is a data-plane design choice, proposed in docs/adr/0346-lane-V-SEC-033-048.md (item 2).

### SEC-038 Prove decompression and pixel bombs are contained

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Operator |

**Spec:** 10 §3.5 (decompression bombs) · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, BE-043

As an operator, I want a tiny hostile file unable to exhaust memory or time, so that an upload cannot take down the asset worker or its neighbours.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given an image whose header declares dimensions beyond the pixel limit, when it is processed, then it fails before pixels are allocated.
- [x] Given multi-frame and animated images with extreme frame counts, when they are processed, then they fail at the limit.
- [x] Given an archive with too many entries, a total size beyond the limit, nested archives, or a compression ratio beyond the limit, when it is processed, then it fails before it is expanded.
- [x] Given a file that hangs the decoder or uses all memory, when the worker's time and memory limits are reached, then the job is killed, the asset is `failed`, `asset.failed` is raised, and other jobs are unaffected.
- [x] Given the limits, when the tests run, then they use the configured values, which are recorded in the operator documentation.

**Evidence:** services/asset-worker/test/content.test.ts (a 50 000 x 50 000 PNG in under 2 KiB, a 65 000 x 65 000 JPEG and a 65 535 x 65 535 GIF fail before allocation, array-buffer growth under 64 MiB; APNG and GIF frame bombs and pixels across frames fail at the limit; archive entry count, total, ratio, lying sizes, overlapping entries and nested archives fail before expansion; every test uses contentLimits()) and test/sandbox.podman.test.ts (the same bombs in the real sandbox; a JPEG at exactly the pixel limit passes and one row more fails). Limits recorded in services/asset-worker/README.md and docs/adr/0043-asset-worker.md. Criterion 4, control-plane half (lane U-SEC-033-048, 2026-11-13): services/document-service/test/control-plane/sec-038-resource-limit.test.ts (memory, one test, passes): a job stopped at its limit settles its asset `failed` with `too_large` and reason `resource_limit`, raises `asset.failed` to the uploader, and a concurrent asset's job settles `ready`. Criterion 4, worker half (lane w8, 2026-11-17): `pnpm exec vitest run --project node services/asset-worker/test/sandbox.podman.test.ts` from the repository root, in the real asset-worker image under Podman on this Windows machine (Podman machine, WSL): 11 passed, 1 skipped (the heavy-file test, which needs DOCEDIT_ASSET_HEAVY_TESTS=1), 186 s. It includes "stops a job at the wall-clock limit", "stops a job at the memory limit" and "fails the job and discards the sandbox when the decoder hangs, exhausts memory, crashes, floods or forges, while another job goes on" (a hanging job settles `resource_limit` / `too_large` and the good job beside it succeeds sooner). The control-plane half was run again the same day: sec-038-resource-limit.test.ts, 1 of 1 passed.

**Notes:** Lane w8 (2026-11-17): criterion 4 ticked. The Podman memory-limit fault recorded by lane U on 2026-11-13 did not occur in this run, so the worker's kill is shown on a real container. The story is done.

**Notes:** The limits are not given in 10 §3.5 or 06 §4.4 (see BE open questions). The values are settings proposed in ADR 0043 (2026-11-06). Criterion 3 is read with 10 §3.5 as edited (E22) and ADR 0017: a Word file's embedded objects are preserved opaquely, so archives under `word/embeddings/` (a chart's workbook, for example) are allowed but never expanded, and an archive anywhere else fails; the owner should confirm this reading. Criterion 4 is met on the worker's side and tested (test/sandbox.podman.test.ts: a decoder that hangs or exhausts memory is killed at the limit, the asset's outcome is `failed` with `resource_limit`, a concurrent job succeeds); it stays open until the control plane raises `asset.failed` from that outcome (PRO-061 criterion 3). Lane U-SEC-033-048 (2026-11-13): the control-plane half is tested (sec-038-resource-limit.test.ts, see Evidence), so the criterion now rests on the worker's kill, which did not run here. `pnpm exec vitest run test/sandbox.podman.test.ts` in services/asset-worker skips all 12 tests: the readiness probe fails because `crun` cannot write `memory.max` in the Podman machine (`podman-machine-default`, WSL, reports cgroups v2 with the systemd manager, and `podman run --memory 256m localhost/docedit-asset-worker:dev` fails with "open `memory.max` for writing: No such file or directory"). This is a host configuration fault that needs the owner or infra to fix (needs in the lane ADR, docs/adr/0346-lane-U-SEC-033-048.md). Criterion stays unticked until that run passes. Lane V-SEC-033-048 (2026-11-13): no run. A probe of `podman run --memory` against the asset worker image did not return within the probe time, while the Podman machine was serving other lanes' containers, so no result exists for this host; left open (docs/adr/0346-lane-V-SEC-033-048.md, item 4).

### SEC-039 Prove DOCX import cannot be abused

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | L | must | Owner |

**Spec:** 10 §3.5 (XML external entities, remote content, macros, impersonation); 09 §12; 07 §15 · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, SEC-059, EXP-098, EXP-099

As an owner, I want an uploaded Word file unable to reach the network, run code or impersonate a colleague, so that importing is as safe as typing.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given XML with a document type declaration, external entities or entity-expansion bombs, when it is imported, then the file is rejected.
- [x] Given external relationships (remote images, linked templates, linked objects), when a file is imported with a canary server listening, then the server sees no request.
- [x] Given a macro-enabled file or a package that contains macro parts, when it is imported, then it is refused.
- [x] Given embedded objects, when a file is imported, then they are kept as opaque preserved content, never parsed, run, rendered or fetched, and the import report lists each as preserved (reworded from "dropped and a warning is returned" by owner decision 153 of 2026-11-15: "kept and reported").
- [x] Given tracked changes whose author is named like an existing user, when they are imported, then the author becomes a label and no attribution goes to that user.
- [x] Given entry names with path traversal and archives beyond the limits on entries, size and ratio, when they are imported, then the file is rejected.
- [x] Given the importer fuzz target of SEC-059, when it runs in CI, then it finds no crash or hang.

**Evidence:** tests/security/test/docx-import-abuse.test.ts (Node.js, run through the real importDocx of packages/export-docx on its sample package). Criterion 1: an internal entity, an external system entity and an entity-expansion bomb in the main part are each refused with ImportError. Criterion 2: three external relationships (image, attached template, hyperlink) point at a canary HTTP server on 127.0.0.1 that counts requests; the control shows the canary counts a real request, and the import makes none. Criterion 3: a main part declared macro-enabled, and a package with a word/vbaProject.bin part, are refused as macro-enabled. Criterion 5: a tracked insertion by a user-like author imports as the `word:` label, and no mark carries the bare identifier. Criterion 6 (lane SEC-027-039, fix of the round 5 review, 2026-11-13; ticked again): docx-import-abuse.test.ts now also imports the sample through `importDocx` with `opts.limits` lowered to one below its entry count, and to one below its declared uncompressed total; each is refused with `ImportError` whose cause is `ZipError` ("more than the limit", "uncompressed size exceeds the limit"), and the same sample with the limit equal to its count or total imports (control). The ratio case (a 64 MiB run of zeros) and the `../escape.xml` entry are refused through `importDocx` in the same file (22 of 22 pass with sec-039-entry-names.test.ts). Also: tests/security/test/sec-039-entry-names.test.ts (Node.js, fast-check; the real `writeZip` and `readZip`): a name with a `..` segment at any position, an absolute name, a drive letter, a backslash and the named cases are refused with `outside the package`, and plain names are read. The `it.fails` of docx-import-abuse.test.ts is now `it`, because `readZip` (packages/export-docx/src/zip.ts, `escapesPackage`) refuses the name. Criterion 7 (lane SEC-027-039, 2026-11-13): tests/security/test/sec-039-importer-fuzz.test.ts (Node.js, fast-check, fixed seed, 150 runs, and 600 runs with `FUZZ_RUNS` also passed) feeds byte flips, truncations, random bytes and XML fragments inserted into the main part through importDocx; a non-`ImportError` exception is a crash, an import over 1 s plus ten times the sample's import time (a test bound, not a spec value) is a hang, and a call to fetch, XMLHttpRequest or WebSocket is refused.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-17; criterion 4 in part, not ticked):** tests/security/test/sec-039-embedded-objects.test.ts (Node.js; 2 tests pass; the real `importDocx` on the POI corpus file `EmbeddedDocument.docx`). Under A2 the file's embedded object is kept as a preserved atom labelled `embedded object`, the import report has one line for it with action `preserved` and the message "preserved, not editable", and no `dropped` line names it; with `fetch`, `XMLHttpRequest` and `WebSocket` replaced by throwing stubs the import calls none of them. The byte-for-byte keeping of the embedding part is packages/export-docx/test/embedded-opaque.test.ts (EXP-099 criterion 6).

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-18, criterion 4, reworded):** the owner's decision 153 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md: active content in imports is preserved, never run, and reported; SEC-039 is reworded to "kept and reported") settles the reading that the 2026-11-17 note below asked for, and the criterion above carries that wording. It is met by tests/security/test/sec-039-embedded-objects.test.ts (2 tests) and packages/export-docx/test/embedded-opaque.test.ts (1 test, EXP-099), `pnpm exec vitest run --project node tests/security/test/sec-039-embedded-objects.test.ts packages/export-docx/test/embedded-opaque.test.ts`, 3 passed on 2026-11-18: the real `importDocx` on the POI corpus file `EmbeddedDocument.docx` keeps the embedded object as a preserved atom labelled `embedded object`, reports one line for it with action `preserved` and the message "preserved, not editable" and none with `dropped`, and calls no `fetch`, `XMLHttpRequest` or `WebSocket`; the embedding part is kept byte for byte, unparsed, in the atom's fragment, which the DOCX writer puts back unchanged. docs/spec/10-security.md (the row "Macros and embedded objects") and 09 section 12 already say this, so no spec change was needed.

**Notes:** Lane w8 (2026-11-18): criterion 4 is reworded as the owner decided (decision 153) and ticked on the Evidence above; all seven criteria are met. Not built, and not part of the reworded criterion: the tenant's option to strip preserved objects (SR-M18; `ImportOptions` has no such setting), and an attack on a client that tries to create preserved data or upload a fragment (SR-C2, named in the second review); both stay as open points for the import and permission stories. Earlier notes follow. Lane SEC-027-039 (2026-11-13): criterion 7 is ticked; the SEC-059 registry still lists `docx-importer` only by name (tests/security/test/fuzz.test.ts), so the run is the new file above and EXP-110 (packages/export-docx/test/fuzz.test.ts). Lane security-2 (2026-11-13): criteria 1, 2, 3 and 5 are ticked. Criterion 6 stays unticked: the entry-name case is a known gap, recorded as `it.fails` in the same file. The zip reader does not refuse a name with a parent-directory segment (`../escape.xml`), and the importer ignores the entry rather than rejecting the file. The ratio limit is tested and refused. Criterion 7 stays unticked (SEC-059 fuzz target). The sample used is docx4j/sample-docx.docx, not a hostile corpus. The test imports across packages by relative path because tests/security has no dependency on @docedit/export-docx; adding one is a package.json change for the main session. Second review (2026-11-06, SPK-027): under A2 embedded objects are kept as opaque preserved content and written back (10 §3.5 as edited, E22), so the fourth criterion changes. SR-M18: preserved embedded objects, external relationships and field codes reach everyone who exports, so the import report lists them and a tenant may strip them; SR-C2: a client must not be able to create preserved data or upload a fragment, which this story should also attack ([review](../review/2026-11-06-second-review.md)). Lane SEC-027-039 (2026-11-13): criterion 4 conflicts with A2, which keeps embedded objects as opaque preserved content; the default used is to leave it unticked and ask the owner to confirm the reading (ADR 0256, item 1). Criteria 1 to 5 of the story are not changed by this batch. Lane SEC-027-039 (2026-11-13, round 5): criterion 6 is ticked; the zip reader now refuses the path-traversal names it used to ignore (packages/export-docx/src/zip.ts, a change in the importer's package, made for this criterion). Review of round 5: unticked, because the entry-count and total-size limits were not tested through the importer. Fix (2026-11-13): both are now refused through `importDocx` with lowered `opts.limits`; criterion 6 is ticked again. The limits are lowered on the sample instead of building a 10,000-entry or 512 MiB archive; the default values (`DEFAULT_ZIP_LIMITS`) are prototype defaults, not spec figures. Lane w8 (2026-11-17): criterion 4 stays unticked. Its wording ("dropped, with a warning") contradicts A2, which the owner settled on 2026-11-06, and docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md does not list it, so the owner has not confirmed the reworded criterion. Proposed wording, following 10 §3.5 as edited: "Given embedded objects, when a file is imported, then each is kept as an opaque preserved atom (never parsed, run, rendered or fetched), the import report lists it as preserved, and it is written back unchanged on DOCX export." The new test proves the import half of that wording. The tenant's option to strip preserved objects (SR-M18, ADR 0256 item 1) is not built: `ImportOptions` has no such setting. Once the owner confirms the reading, reword the criterion and tick it on the new test and EXP-099.

### SEC-040 Prove decoder exploits and type confusion are contained

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Viewer |

**Spec:** 10 §3.5 (decoder exploits, content-type confusion) · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, BE-043, BE-045

As a viewer, I want a hostile image unable to harm me or the application when it is displayed, so that images are as safe as text.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the asset worker, when it is probed, then it has no network route and no credentials, and runs in a sandbox.
- [x] Given a polyglot file (a valid image with an appended script or HTML), when it is processed, then the stored object is the re-encoded output and contains no appended payload.
- [x] Given a declared type that differs from the detected type, when the asset is processed, then it fails.
- [x] Given a stored asset, when it is served, then the response has a fixed content type for its kind and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, from an origin separate from the application's.
- [x] Given the asset origin is opened in a browser, when scripts there run, then they cannot read the application origin's storage or cookies.

**Evidence:** services/asset-worker. Criterion 1: test/sandbox.podman.test.ts probes. Criterion 2: test/content.test.ts (PNG, JPEG and GIF with HTML, scripts or a ZIP archive appended), test/worker.test.ts, test/sandbox.podman.test.ts ('a PNG polyglot loses its payload'). Criterion 3: test/content.test.ts ('fails a declared type that differs from the detected one'), test/worker.test.ts, test/s3.test.ts. Criterion 4: test/origin.test.ts and test/s3.test.ts (fixed content type, nosniff, sandboxing policy, separate origin). Criterion 5: test/origin-isolation.test.ts (Chromium; application on 127.0.0.1, asset origin on localhost: no script of a hostile SVG or of HTML served as PNG runs, the same SVG without the headers does run, and a script on the asset origin reads neither the application's storage nor its cookies).

**Notes:** Lane U-SEC-033-048 (2026-11-13): test/sandbox.podman.test.ts skips all 12 tests on this host (the sandbox's memory limit does not start; see docs/adr/0346-lane-U-SEC-033-048.md). Criterion 1 and the Podman-backed parts of criteria 2 and 3 therefore rest on an earlier run that this lane could not repeat.

## Signing and evidence

### SEC-041 Prove signing keys never reach content workers

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | M | must | Relying party |

**Spec:** 10 §3.6 (stolen signing key); 12 §8.2; ADR 0013 · **Requirements:** R13, R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, FRW-052, SPK-021

As a relying party, I want the key that seals documents out of reach of anything that touches user content, so that a compromised worker cannot seal what it likes.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given the signing service, when its memory, configuration and environment are inspected, then no private key material is present; signing happens in a hardware security module or a key service.
- [ ] Given the export, asset, import and indexer workers, when they try to reach the key service or the signing service, then the attempt fails by network policy and credentials.
- [x] Given the signing service, when it is deployed, then it is a separate deployable with its own credentials, not shared with any worker that processes user content.
- [x] Given each signing operation, when it happens, then it is recorded in the audit trail with the document hash and the requester.
- [x] Given a key rotation, when it happens, then seals made earlier still validate.

**Evidence:** No criterion ticked (reviewer, round 3, 2026-11-13: criterion 5 unticked, see Notes). services/document-service/src/storage/key-rotation-seals.test.ts (lane SEC-040-052, 2026-11-13; Node.js, fast-check; `MemoryKeyService` of key-service.ts). Building block for criterion 5: a seal made under a `sign` key validates after the key is replaced by a new one and disabled, under its public JWK and after the disable; the old key then refuses to sign; a seal of the new key does not validate under the old key (one case, and a property over one to four rotations and every generation's seal).

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-26, criteria 3, 4 and 5):** Criterion 4: services/signing-service/test/frw-052-service.test.ts ("the audit entries hold identifiers and hashes", written before the key is used and after the seal, none when the log cannot be written; passes) and sec-041-rotation.test.ts (below): each sealing has a `seal.requested` and a `seal.sealed` entry with the caller the credential names (`document-service`), the document identifier, `seq`, the SHA-256 of the PDF, the key identifier and the hash of the sealed bytes, and no secret. Criterion 5: services/signing-service/test/sec-041-rotation.test.ts (Node.js, OpenSSL, test PKIs and time-stamp authorities; 1 test passes, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node services/signing-service/test/sec-041-rotation.test.ts`): a seal made under `operator-2026` is checked with the seal verifier of FRW-055 (`verifySealedPdf`, and OpenSSL) after the service is configured with a new key `operator-2027` (its own certificate chain and time-stamp authority) and the old key service is out of its key directory: it validates under the old root, no call reached the old key after the rotation, the new seal validates under the new root and not under the old root alone, and the old seal does not validate under the new root alone (it was not re-signed); the trail holds both sealings in order with their own key identifiers. Criterion 3: services/signing-service/test/sec-041-worker-isolation.podman.test.ts (Podman machine route, Windows 11; 5 tests pass with `DOCEDIT_SIGNING_CONTAINER=1`, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node services/signing-service/test/sec-041-worker-isolation.podman.test.ts`; it reuses the image `docedit-signing-service:test-w4` of FRW-052 and builds one of its own when that is missing): the image holds `services/signing-service` and the packages `authority corpus model rules` and no worker or other service, runs as a user that is not root, and answers only with its own configuration; none of its credentials (the two secrets of the test configuration) is in the export worker's image or the asset worker's image; and the container sits on a network of its own with no published port.

**Notes:** Lane w8 (2026-11-26): criteria 3, 4 and 5 are ticked; criteria 1 and 2 stay unticked. Criterion 5 is ticked on this lane's reading, which the owner has not confirmed: a rotation is a new key with its own certificate chain, the service's operator key identifier changed to it and the old key retired from the key directory. The spec and ADR 0013 say nothing of a rotation operation, and the earlier note asked for one in product code; there is none beyond the service's configuration, the key directory and the verifier, which are product code. If the owner wants an explicit operation (for example a `rotate` call of the key service with an overlap period for time-stamp renewal), the tick is to be reviewed. Criterion 2 shows the export worker's image and the asset worker's image: from the workers' network there is no connection to the signing container (no route between the two Podman networks), from the signing network the export worker's own credential is refused 403 (wrong caller) and a made-up or absent one 401. It stays unticked because the import and indexer workers are not separate images in this tree (they run as job handlers of the document service, so there is no worker container to probe), and because the key service of the development emulator (SoftHSM2 in Podman, owner decision 222) is reached by `podman exec`, not over a network, so a network policy for it is the deployment's. Criterion 1 stays unticked: the same test inspects the running signing container's environment, command line and files for private key material and finds none, and the service's sources and the compile side's are scanned in spk-021-no-key-on-worker.test.ts, but the memory of the process is not inspected and the module is the development emulator, not a hardware security module or a cloud key service (FRW-052 criterion 2, the owner's choice of a production module, decision 221 to 223 allow the development emulator only). The earlier note that no signing service exists is out of date (services/signing-service).

**Notes:** Reviewer (round 3, 2026-11-13): criterion 5 unticked. The ADR 0013 seal is a PAdES signature by a separate signing service; none of that exists, the key service has no rotation operation, and the test's `validates` helper (WebCrypto verify) lives in the test, so the test shows only that `disable` keeps the public JWK. Tick when a rotation operation and a seal verifier exist in product code. Lane SEC-040-052 (2026-11-13) had ticked criterion 5 on the key service's public half, which stays published after a disable; the rotation is a create-then-disable by the caller, not a rotation operation of the service. The seal format of the export is not built, so the test checks a raw ECDSA signature. Criteria 1 (no private key in the signing service, inspected in memory, configuration and environment), 2 (network policy and credentials for the workers), 3 (a separate deployable with its own credentials) and 4 (audit of each signing with document hash and requester) stay unticked: the signing service is in-process (`MemoryKeyService`, the same process as the document service), there is no network policy, deployment or audit trail for signing, and the HSM or key service is not built. Lane w8 (2026-11-17): blocked, nothing ticked and nothing built. A search of services/ and packages/ finds no signing service, no deployable for one, no key service client for workers, no network policy and no rotation operation: `KeyService` (services/document-service/src/storage/key-service.ts) has `create`, `publicJwk`, `sign`, `unwrap`, `enabled` and `disable`, and the sealing code in packages/authority/src/workflow/sealing.ts holds the pure rules only (`selectSealingKey`, `checkSealRequest`). FRW-052 (the signing service, its trust boundary, its audit log and its per-tenant key choice) is unticked in every criterion, and SPK-021 (a sealed sample PDF with a managed key) is blocked, so criteria 1 to 4 have nothing to inspect, probe, deploy or audit. Criterion 5 needs a rotation operation and a seal verifier in product code, which wait on the same service. Building a stand-in service here would only test the stand-in. Resume once FRW-052 has a service with its own credentials: the checks are an inspection of its memory, configuration and environment for private key material (criterion 1), a probe from each worker's container that must fail (criterion 2, Podman with a network policy), the deployment descriptor (criterion 3), the audit rows with the document hash and requester (criterion 4), and a seal verified after a rotation (criterion 5).

### SEC-042 Prove a tampered PDF cannot be sealed

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | M | must | Relying party |

**Spec:** 10 §3.6 (a tampered PDF being sealed), §3.4; 12 §8.2; 09 §9.3 (8) · **Requirements:** R13, R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-035, FRW-052, EXP-068, EXP-070

As a relying party, I want a sealed PDF to be exactly what the log says was agreed, so that a compromised worker cannot slip a changed page into a signed document.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a final export, when it is prepared for sealing, then the bundle is pinned to the completion `seq` and chain head, and a bundle whose manifest does not match the log is refused.
- [x] Given two independent workers compile the same bundle, when one is made to change a single byte or one invisible character in its output, then the comparison fails, the seal is refused, and the job fails with a diagnostic.
- [x] Given a sealed PDF is stored, when a second write to its key is attempted, then it is refused.
- [x] Given the hash sent to the signing service, when it is compared with the stored PDF, then they are equal.
- [x] Given long-term validation data is added later, when it is added, then it is added only as incremental updates and the sealed bytes are not rewritten.

**Evidence:** tests/security/test/sealed-write-once.test.ts (Node.js, fast-check; `AssetStore.putSealed` and `getSealed` over `MemoryObjectStore`, the store of services/document-service). Criterion 3: a second `putSealed` to the key of a sealed PDF is refused with `ObjectExistsError` and the first bytes are read back unchanged, for a fixed case and for any two byte strings (property, 100 runs); a control shows that another workflow or another tenant may hold its own sealed PDF, so the refusal is by key.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-17; criteria 1 and 2):** tests/security/test/sec-042-tampered-pdf.test.ts (Node.js, fast-check; 9 tests pass). It runs the export worker's real job handler (services/export-worker/src/job.ts) through the real `JobRunner` and queue, with a fake compile that records its calls, as services/export-worker/src/exp-079-sealing.job.test.ts does. Criterion 2: with the second compile (and, in a second property, the first) returning a PDF with any one byte changed (40 and 20 runs), or with one invisible character (zero-width space, zero-width non-joiner, soft hyphen, byte order mark) inserted at any position (40 runs), the job fails with `seal_pair_differs`, its diagnostic carries the hash of the first compile, nothing is stored (no `output.pdf`, no manifest), and both compiles ran under two sandbox names; the control with two equal compiles completes, stores the PDF and records both hashes. `comparePair` of a differing pair gives no sealable PDF, `acceptForSealing` throws for it and for a hand-built object of the same shape, and a pair in one container is refused. Criterion 1, worker side: a pin of another seq or another chain head, a log whose chain head at the seq is another one, and a bundle with no chain head (the log gives none, or `chainHeadAt` is not set) are each refused with `seal_pin_mismatch` before anything is compiled or stored; the matching pin is compiled twice. Also services/export-worker/src/exp-079-sealing.job.test.ts and exp-079-pair.test.ts (EXP-079 criteria 2 to 4).

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-26; criteria 4 and 5):** tests/security/test/sec-042-sealed-bytes.test.ts (Node.js 22.18, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, 5 tests pass, none skipped; OpenSSL, a local OpenSSL time-stamp authority, the key in the test process, memory storage). It runs the live path of finalisation with nothing called by hand (the last participant completes, the export job stores a PDF, the seal step reads it from the object store and sends it to the real signing service of FRW-052 over HTTP); the document service's client has a `fetch` that records each request body. Criterion 4: the one `/v1/seal` request has `pdfSha256` equal to the SHA-256 of `output.pdf` read back from the object store by its key, equal to both compile hashes and to the hash of the base64 bytes it carries, and the sealed PDF in the sealed store begins with exactly those bytes (hash of that prefix equal to the hash sent) and validates under OpenSSL. When the object store holds a PDF that differs by one bit from the one the pair agreed on, no seal request is sent, the workflow stays `completed` and nothing is stored. The signing service itself refuses (an error status, not `ok`) a request whose `pdfSha256` is the hash of other bytes and one whose pair of compile hashes is not both the PDF's hash; the control with matching hashes seals. Criterion 5: `extendFinalised` appends validation data and a time-stamp; the sealed object stays byte for byte, the update has those bytes as its first bytes, the original signature and the document time-stamp both validate under OpenSSL and `verifySealedPdf`; an answer from the service that rewrites a byte inside the sealed bytes is refused with `not_an_append` and no update is stored. Also services/signing-service/test/frw-056-extend.test.ts and services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-053.live-seal.test.ts (FRW-056 criteria 1 and 3).

**Notes (lane w8, 2026-11-26):** Criteria 4 and 5 are ticked now that the signing service (FRW-052), the live seal path (FRW-053) and the incremental-update writer (FRW-056 criteria 1 and 3) exist. Limits that stand: the key is in the test process, so the test shows the rules and not the key's place (FRW-052 criterion 2 is open for the owner's choice of a hardware module or key service, decision 222); the compiles are still a fake that returns equal hashes (see the earlier note, limit c); the memory object store stands in for the one with Object Lock, whose refusal of a second write is proved in sealed-write-once.test.ts.

**Notes:** Lane security-2 (2026-11-13): criterion 3 is ticked. It is proved on the object store's write-once write that `putSealed` makes; the test does not run the export job. Criteria 1 (the bundle pinned to the completion `seq` and chain head, refused on a manifest that does not match the log), 2 (two independent workers compared byte for byte), 4 (the hash sent to the signing service equal to the stored PDF) and 5 (incremental long-term validation data) stay unticked: the completion log, a second compile worker and the signing service are not built. `verifyBundle` in services/export-worker checks a bundle's manifest against its files, not against the log. Lane w8 (2026-11-17): criteria 1 and 2 are ticked on the worker's side, in the reading that lane w2 used for EXP-079 criteria 2 and 3. Limits: (a) the control plane does not yet write the `sealing` pin into the job payload (FRW-049, FRW-053), so the pin in the test is given, and the manifest of the bundle is compared with it, not with a stored completion record; the worker's `chainHeadAt` is the log read it uses. (b) The two compiles are two sandboxes under two names started by one worker; what "independent workers" must mean (two processes, hosts or images) is open in EXP-079 criterion 1 and is the owner's to say. (c) The compile is a fake that returns the changed bytes; a real compile cannot be made to change one byte on demand, so the test shows the comparison, the refusal and the diagnostic, not that a real compromised worker is caught. (d) The job cuts its diagnostic message, so the second hash is not always in it. Criteria 4 (the hash sent to the signing service against the stored PDF) and 5 (incremental updates only) stay unticked: there is no signing service (FRW-052) and no incremental-update writer (FRW-056 criteria 1 and 3 are open).

### SEC-043 Prove product language does not over-claim

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | S | should | Relying party |

**Spec:** 10 §3.6 (claims about signer identity); 12 §8.3; ADR 0013 · **Requirements:** R13 · **Depends on:** FRW-040, FRW-050, SPK-024

As a relying party, I want the product never to promise more than a simple electronic signature with evidence, so that nobody relies on a claim the platform cannot support.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the interface strings, documentation, API descriptions, the certificate of completion template and marketing pages in the repository, when a language check runs in CI, then phrases such as "qualified" and "legally binding everywhere" are flagged unless they appear in a reviewed context that says the product does not provide them.
- [ ] Given the certificate of completion and the evidence record, when they show how a participant was authenticated, then they present the host's claims (`amr`, `auth_time`) as assertions recorded as given, not as verified identity.
- [ ] Given the wording, when it is reviewed, then counsel's review from the legal note SPK-024 is recorded before launch.

**Evidence:** tests/security/test/language-check.test.ts (Node.js). Criterion 1: the check scans the product text under packages, services, apps, infra and docs, plus PRODUCT.md and README.md, for "qualified" as a signature, seal or time-stamp level, "legally binding everywhere" and the other phrases of the legal note's word lists. A match passes only when its line negates it ("never", "not", "without" and similar) or its file is a reviewed record (the spec, ADRs, stories, review notes and the legal note). The test has a control set of sentences and a check that the scan reads more than 100 files; the repository scan finds nothing.

**Notes:** 12 §8.3 forbids the wording; the legal review is outside the repository. Lane security-2 (2026-11-13): criterion 1 is ticked. The check runs under `pnpm test`; wiring it into the CI workflow is in `.github`, which this lane does not edit (needs the main session). Test sources (`*.test.ts`, `*.spec.ts`) are not scanned, because their phrases are test data. The word list and the reviewed-record rule are a proposed design, recorded in docs/adr/0155-security-2-backlog-batch.md. Criterion 2 stays unticked: the certificate of completion template and the evidence record are not built (FRW-040, FRW-050). Criterion 3 stays unticked: counsel's review is outside the repository. Lane w8 (2026-11-17), nothing new ticked: `packages/authority/src/workflow/certificate.ts` now exists and its statement says the authentication is asserted by the host and the platform does not verify identity, but the certificate shows no `amr` or `auth_time` values, and the evidence record of FRW-050 is not checked against criterion 2 here; the owner's decisions of 2026-11-15 do not change this story. Criterion 3 needs counsel. Status: blocked. `tests/security/test/language-check.test.ts` passes (criterion 1). Lane w8 (2026-11-26, round 6): nothing new ticked. Criterion 2 is conditional ("when they show how a participant was authenticated"): the certificate of `packages/authority/src/workflow/certificate.ts` says authentication is asserted by the host but shows no `amr` or `auth_time`, and the evidence record (`participant-evidence.ts`) was not checked against it in this round, so the criterion's condition is not decided here. Criterion 3 needs counsel (FRW-087); no decision of docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md covers either.

### SEC-044 Prove evidence stays outside the log

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | M | must | Participant |

**Spec:** 10 §3.6 (evidence leaking or outliving its purpose); 12 §7; 08 §2 · **Requirements:** R13, R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-029, FRW-040, FRW-050

As a participant, I want my IP address and browser details kept in a store that can be deleted on schedule, so that signing does not put them in an immutable record.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a completed workflow, when the commit log, control commits, audit trail, logs and search entries are scanned, then no IP address, user agent, `amr`, consent text or signature image appears; only salted hashes of evidence appear in the chain.
- [x] Given evidence reaches the end of its retention period, when it is deleted, then the hash chain still verifies.
- [x] Given a tenant switches off IP address and user agent capture, when a participant signs, then neither is stored.
- [x] Given the salt, when the chain is read, then it is not stored in the log.
- [x] Given a legal hold on the document, when the retention period ends, then the evidence is kept until the hold ends.

**Evidence:** tests/security/test/evidence-outside-log.test.ts (Node.js, fast-check; `planPurge` of packages/authority/src/workflow/retention.ts, and `controlCommitEvidence`, `commitEvidence` and `verifyEvidence` of packages/authority/src/workflow/evidence.ts). Criterion 5 (unticked by the reviewer, round 3: `planPurge` is not exported from packages/authority/src/index.ts and nothing but tests imports it, so no product code keeps evidence under a hold yet; the test proves only the pure decision): with the hold on, a row past its retention period is kept and the audit records no removal; once the hold is lifted the same rows are removed. For any rows, retention and instant, a held document keeps every row and, without the hold, exactly the expired rows are removed. Criterion 5 (lane SEC-040-052, 2026-11-13): tests/security/test/evidence-purge-job.test.ts runs the purge job (packages/authority/src/workflow/evidence-purge-job.ts, `runEvidencePurge`, which calls `planPurge` and deletes only the rows the plan names) over an in-memory store: under a hold nothing is deleted and the audit records no removal; once the hold ends the expired rows are removed; a property over 300 runs. Also (chain part, not a criterion ticked here): the control commit payload has exactly its origin and a salted hash, with no IP address, `amr`, consent text or salt in it, for any IP address and salt (property).

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-17; criterion 2 ticked, criteria 1, 3 and 4 in part):** tests/security/test/sec-044-evidence-scan.pg.test.ts (Node.js, PostgreSQL in the shared Podman container, its own database dropped afterwards; 1 test passes, not skipped). A signing's evidence is recorded through the product path `PgCaptureSettingsStore.recordEvidence` beside a real commit log of three commits, with one canary per class: an IP address, a user agent, an `amr` value, a consent text and a signature image. Every base table is then searched as text by the admin role, so the commit log, the audit trail and any search table are covered. With capture on, each canary is in `workflow_evidence` and in no other table. After the tenant switches capture off (through `change`, which audits it: the entry names the actor), a later event's IP address and user agent are in no table at all and its `amr` is kept in `workflow_evidence` only. After `runEvidencePurge` (`PgEvidenceRowStore`, `PgPurgeAuditSink`) removes the two rows past their retention, no canary of any class is in any table, and `logs.verify` of the log answers as before the purge (`{ ok: true, head: 3 }`). Criterion 2 is also shown by services/document-service/test/postgres/frw-043.v-frw-037-054.purge-sealed.pg.test.ts (the same purge, the commit log, head and chain verify unchanged, the sealed PDF untouched; run again in this lane: passes).

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-18; criteria 3 and 4 ticked, criterion 1 in part):** tests/security/test/sec-044-w8-workflow-scan.test.ts (Node.js 22.18, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node <file>`, 2 of 2 pass, memory and PostgreSQL in the lane's own Podman container `docedit-test-w8-postgres`, none skipped). The real service with the real data plane and the asset worker; the participants use only the endpoints: a WebSocket view (first signer from `127.0.0.77` with a unique user agent and `amr`), `POST .../consent`, an asset upload and `POST /fields/{fid}/sign`, `POST .../complete`. Criterion 3: the tenant switches capture off between the two signers (memory: the ledger's setting; PostgreSQL: `PgCaptureSettingsStore.change`, one `capture.setting.changed` entry in `audit_events`). The second signer's rows for the view, consent, signing and completion hold no `ipAddress` and no `userAgent`, and their address (`127.0.0.78`) and agent are in no table at all; the other items (their `amr`) are kept. The first signer's rows, made with capture on, hold the address and agent, so the check can fail. Criterion 4: the hex of every stored salt (the run stored at least eight rows) is in no commit of the log, no head, no object of the store, no event and no line of the service log, and on PostgreSQL in no table but `workflow_evidence`. Criterion 1, the part that is shown: neither signer's address, user agent, `amr` or the consent text, nor the base64 of the signature image, is in a commit, the head, an object, an event or a service log line; on PostgreSQL, searched as text and as the hex of bytes in every base table, the first signer's address, agent and `amr` are in `workflow_evidence` and no other table, the consent text and the image bytes are in none. The scan found nothing outside the evidence table on the first run.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-26; criterion 1 ticked):** the same test, extended (tests/security/test/sec-044-w8-workflow-scan.test.ts, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, 2 of 2 pass, memory and PostgreSQL in the lane's own Podman project `docedit-test-w8` on ports 55832 and 57870, none skipped). The chain: the log of the two-signer run holds at least four control commits with an `evidence` member (origin `sign` or `rules`); each has no steps and exactly the keys `origin` and `evidenceHash` (64 hex digits), and each hash is the salted hash of one stored row, recomputed from the row and its salt with `recomputeCommitment` (FRW-041 criterion 2 is built now, so this is the "only salted hashes" half of the criterion). The earlier scans stand (commits, head, objects, events, service log, every PostgreSQL table as text and as hex, the audit trail of the capture switch). Search entries: the service runs with `DOCEDIT_SEARCH_INDEX=1`; the text the indexer extracts (`extractSearchText`) from the workflow document's content after both signers holds no canary of any class; a sibling document of the tenant is checkpointed and found by a word of its text (the control that the index works), and a search for each canary (address, agent and `amr` of both signers, the consent text) finds neither document; on PostgreSQL the index tables are no longer empty and the base-table scan finds the canaries only in `workflow_evidence`.

**Notes (lane w8, 2026-11-26):** Criterion 1 is ticked now that the control commit with the evidence hash (FRW-041 criterion 2) and the search index (BE-054) exist. One limit: the workflow document's own content cannot be replaced to force a checkpoint of it (the service answers 409 to the content replacement while a workflow holds the document), so its search entry is not read; the text the indexer would extract from it is, and the indexer takes text from the checkpoint content and nothing else. All five criteria are ticked.

**Notes:** Lane security-2 (2026-11-13): criterion 5 was ticked on the purge decision only and is unticked by the reviewer (round 3): the purge job that deletes rows is not built, `planPurge` deletes nothing and is wired into no service; the test proves the decision the job must follow. Tick it when the job calls `planPurge` and a test runs the job under a hold. Lane SEC-040-052 (2026-11-13): the job is built as a new file (`runEvidencePurge` in packages/authority/src/workflow/evidence-purge-job.ts; the store and the audit sink are interfaces) and criterion 5 is ticked on its test. The job is not scheduled and is wired into no service, and no stored evidence table sits behind the store interface; the scheduling and the service wiring are the workflow service's. The retention period and the start of the retention clock are open (FRW-084), so the test passes both explicitly. Criterion 1 (the scan of the commit log, audit trail, logs and search entries) stays unticked: the stored log and the workflow tables are not built. Criterion 2 (the hash chain still verifies after deletion) stays unticked: the chain is not built. Criterion 3 (the tenant switch for IP and user-agent capture) stays unticked: no capture is built. Criterion 4 (the salt is not stored in the log, when the chain is read) stays unticked: the stored chain is not built; the payload built by `controlCommitEvidence` never holds the salt, which the test shows. Lane V-SEC-033-048 (2026-11-13): tests/security/test/sec-044-v-payload-salt.test.ts (Node.js, fast-check, 2 of 2 passed) serialises the control commit's payload as a log would hold it and finds no salt in hex, base64 or decimal and no canonical text, for any input; it also checks that the commitment still verifies with the evidence and the salt. Criterion 4 stays unticked: no chain or log of commits is stored. Lane w8 (2026-11-17): criterion 2 is ticked on the two PostgreSQL tests above: evidence is deleted by the product's purge job and the stored chain verifies unchanged. The chain holds no evidence commitment yet (no control commit is built from `controlCommitEvidence`, and `StoredCommit` has no field for one), so the verify shows that deletion touches nothing the chain covers, not that a commitment still verifies. Criteria 1, 3 and 4 stay unticked. Criterion 1 names a completed workflow, and the scan of logs and search entries and the check that only salted hashes appear in the chain have no workflow to run on: there is no participant endpoint that views, consents, signs or completes (FRW-038, FRW-040 criterion 2), no workflow service writes control commits with evidence hashes (FRW-041 criterion 2), and `recordEvidence` is called by tests only. The new test scans every table, but it hands the facts to `recordEvidence` itself, and it does not scan service logs (SEC-047) or object-store files. Criterion 3 names a participant who signs: the setting's effect on stored evidence is shown (the later event holds neither value anywhere), but the signing endpoint is missing, as FRW-042 criterion 1 records. Criterion 4 needs a stored chain that carries evidence commitments, which does not exist. Tick 1, 3 and 4 when the participant endpoints and the control commit with the evidence hash exist: the new test is the place to drive them (replace the direct `recordEvidence` call with the endpoint, and read the control commit's payload for the hash and for the absence of the salt). Lane w8 (2026-11-18), with the participant endpoints now built (FRW-040, FRW-044 to FRW-047): tests/security/test/sec-044-w8-workflow-scan.test.ts drives them (two signers, each viewing over a WebSocket from a loopback address of its own with its own user agent and `amr`, accepting the consent text, uploading a PNG, signing and completing) and ticks criteria 3 and 4. Criterion 1 stays unticked for two reasons. First, "only salted hashes of evidence appear in the chain" cannot be read: no control commit carries an evidence hash (FRW-041 criterion 2; ADR 0397 puts the hash in a later control commit that is not built, and `StoredCommit` has no member for it), so the chain holds no hash of evidence at all. Second, the search entries are not scanned with content that a workflow could put there, because the index is fed from checkpoints (BE-054), which are not built; the PostgreSQL scan covers the index table, which is empty of these canaries. The rest of criterion 1 is shown by the same test (no canary in the commits, objects, events, service log or, on PostgreSQL, any table but `workflow_evidence`). Criterion 4 is ticked against the commits that exist (two `sign` commits and the control commits of the run), not against a commitment, because none is written yet: the test fails if a later change writes a salt into the log, and it is the place to add the check that a commit's hash matches `recomputeCommitment` when FRW-041 criterion 2 is built. The condition for ticking criterion 1 is the control commit with the evidence hash.

## Data and tenancy

### SEC-045 Prove one tenant cannot reach another's data

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1a | L | must | Tenant administrator |

**Spec:** 10 §3.7 (first bullet), §1 (tenant isolation); 06 §4.1, §12 · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, BE-011, BE-012, BE-003

As a tenant administrator, I want proof that another customer can never reach my documents, history, files or jobs, so that sharing a service is safe.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given two tenants with documents, users, threads, exports, assets, webhooks, jobs and search entries, when every REST route and every WebSocket message that takes an identifier is called with tenant A's credentials and tenant B's identifiers, then each is refused and returns no data.
- [x] Given the database catalogue, when a test lists every table, then each has a `tenant_id`, row-level security enabled and forced, and a policy; a new table without them fails the build.
- [x] Given a handler written without a tenant filter (a deliberate test fault), when it runs under tenant A, then it sees no rows of tenant B.
- [x] Given a full scenario, when every object key written is listed, then each starts with `tenants/{tenant}/`, and a signed URL for tenant A's object does not work on a key of tenant B.
- [ ] Given every cache, counter and stream (rendered versions, content reads, idempotency keys, rate-limit counters, search entries, bus streams, Redis or Valkey keys), when their keys are listed, then each includes the tenant.
- [x] Given per-tenant encryption keys, when an object of tenant A is read with the key of tenant B, then it cannot be decrypted.
- [ ] Given tenant-supplied fonts, when tenant B's export job loads fonts, then tenant A's fonts are unavailable.
