**Evidence:** test/tenant-isolation.test.ts (memory and PostgreSQL): tenant A service credential is refused (403/404) with no data on every document, content, commits, regions and grants route of tenant B, and an A-minted token naming B document cannot read it over REST or open its channel (not_found); object keys are namespaced by tenant; on PostgreSQL every table with tenant_id has row-level security enabled, forced and a policy, and the core tables are tenant-scoped. Round 6 (criterion 6): test/tenant-objects.test.ts seals tenant A's object, copies its bytes under tenant B's prefix and checks that B's key cannot open them (and that a move within a tenant fails, since the object key is the additional data), that B's unwrap key refuses A's wrapped data key, and, for any body (fast-check), that a moved object yields no plaintext; A's own reads still work.

**Evidence (lane U-SEC-033-048, 2026-11-13, criterion 3):** tests/security/test/sec-045-unfiltered-handler.test.ts, run with Vitest against PostgreSQL in the shared Podman container (`docedit-test-postgres`) in its own database, dropped afterwards (5 of 5 passed). The probe table takes the migrations' `docedit_protect`, and a query with no tenant filter, run as the service role under tenant A, returns only A's rows; the control and the no-tenant case also hold, and an unfiltered update as A changes only A's row.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-15, criterion 1):** tests/security/test/sec-045-route-sweep.test.ts, run with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node tests/security/test/sec-045-route-sweep.test.ts` on the memory harness (8 passed; the 4 PostgreSQL variants were skipped, PostgreSQL not available on this machine). The suite reads `spec/dep/dep-1.openapi.json` and calls each of the 56 operations whose path has a parameter with identifiers of objects tenant B created (document, region, thread, comment, version, export job, asset, webhook, hold), once with A's API key and once with an A-signed user token naming B's document as owner: each is refused with 401, 403 or 404, carries no canary text or identifier of B, and B's objects read the same afterwards. A path parameter without an identifier fails the test. "the control" shows tenant B gets past the tenant check on 41 of them with its own credentials; the other 15 have no handler in this build (see Notes). "the tenant-wide list and query routes" checks every read without a path parameter. "classifies every message a client can send (AsyncAPI)" and "tenant A's WebSocket messages that take an identifier reach nothing of tenant B" cover `ch.open`, `sync.submit`, `presence.update`, `ch.close`, `auth.refresh` and `chunk`, with B's own session watching and B's sequence number unchanged.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-26; criterion 4 ticked, criteria 5 and 7 in part):** tests/security/test/sec-045-w8-keys-and-urls.test.ts (Node.js 22.18, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, 3 tests pass, none skipped; the S3-compatible store of the test environment, project `docedit-test-w8`, port 57870). Keys: two tenants each run a scenario that writes every kind of object the service keeps (a content replacement with its checkpoints, an uploaded and checked asset, a completed export job with output and log, a tenant font, a sealed final and the anchor of a workflow); every key of the object store starts with the prefix of the tenant that wrote it, each tenant has a key of each kind, a key that names one tenant's document is under that tenant's prefix, and A's font hash is not a key under B's prefix. `TenantObjects` signs no URL for a key of another tenant and none for a traversal (`ForeignKeyError`). Signed URL: on the real store, a URL signed for tenant A's object opens it (200, A's bytes); the same signature and expiry moved to the path of tenant B's object are refused by the store (status of 400 or more, B's bytes not in the answer); B's own URL opens B's object. Criterion 7, the part the service serves: B's `GET /v1/fonts` holds no trace of A's family or hash, and `GET /v1/tenant-fonts/{sha}` of A's hash is 404 for B (also services/document-service/test/control-plane/be-048-tenant-fonts.test.ts). Criterion 5: tests/security/test/sec-045-w8-key-inventory.test.ts (4 tests pass). Every key of the shared rate store (`t:{tenant}`, `u:{tenant}\0{user}`) holds the tenant, recorded through a `RateStore` that logs each key; the idempotency records of two tenants with one key are two records; the pinned read cache keys a request by its tenant. Two kinds of key hold no tenant and are pinned in the test: the address counters (`c:` and `f:`, kept in the node's memory and never sent to the shared store) and the bus topics (`node:{node}` and `announce`, found by a scan of every `publish` and `subscribe` call).

**Notes (lane w8, 2026-11-26):** Criterion 4 is ticked: the assets, exports, fonts and seals it waited on exist now. The memory store's own signed URL (`memory://`, an HMAC over the key) has no verifier, so the URL half is shown on the S3-compatible store, whose signature covers the path; the asset origin's verifier is tested in sec-045-signed-url-tenant.test.ts. Criterion 5 stays unticked: the node inbox streams and the `announce` topic hold no tenant (ADR 0393 chose one inbox stream per node), and the address counters cannot (no tenant is known before a token is verified). Whether that is an accepted exception or the keys must change is the owner's: docs/adr/0428-w8-SEC-045-key-inventory.md (proposed, options A to C). No Valkey server runs on this machine (the compose file of the test environment has none), so the keys are listed from the product's own key builders and call sites, not read from a server. Criterion 7 stays unticked: the export worker takes its font manifest from its image and no tenant's job mounts a tenant font yet (EXP-048 criteria 1 and 2 are open), so there is no job of tenant B that loads fonts to test; what the service serves is covered above.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-15): criterion 1 is ticked on the memory run of the route sweep. Its limit, stated plainly: 15 operations of the OpenAPI document have no handler in the document service of this build (`listHistory`, `getDiff`, `getAttribution`, `listThreads`, `createImport`, `getImport`, the eight workflow operations, and `signField`); tenant B itself gets 404 "this server does not offer the route" on them, so the refusal of tenant A there shows only that nothing answers. The sweep pins that list (`NOT_FOUND_FOR_OWNER`): when one of them gets a handler the control test fails until the operation is taken off the list, and it is then attacked like the rest. The scenario has no import job, workflow or search entry of tenant B, because those features are not served. The PostgreSQL variant of the sweep has not been run. Criteria 4, 5 and 7 are unchanged: the service has no signed-URL verifier of its own, the caches and counters of criterion 5 are not all built, and tenant-supplied fonts are not built. Optional dedicated deployments (BE-093) run the same suite. The suite grows as features land: webhooks, search and exports join when they exist. Round 6: criterion 6 is ticked. Lane V-SEC-033-048 (round 7, criterion 1, still unticked): tests/security/test/sec-045-v-b-threads.test.ts (memory and PostgreSQL, 2 of 2 pass with `pnpm exec vitest run test/sec-045-v-b-threads.test.ts` in tests/security) calls tenant A's service credential and an A user token on the thread and comment identifier routes (`/threads/{tid}`, `/threads/{tid}/comments`, `/comments/{cid}`) and on B's export list with B's identifiers, and finds each refused with 401, 403 or 404 and no B canary. Still open for criterion 1: the webhook, font and legal-hold routes (the default harness does not offer them) and the WebSocket messages beyond `open`. Lane V-SEC-033-048 (2026-11-13), criterion 1 (partial, unticked): tests/security/test/sec-045-v-b-identifiers.test.ts (memory and PostgreSQL, 2 of 2 pass with `pnpm exec vitest run` at the root) calls tenant A's credential on 16 REST routes with tenant B's identifiers (fields, field values, asset resolve, exports and export jobs, fork, versions, threads, history, attribution, diff, restore, read marker, retention) and finds each refused with 401, 403 or 404 and no B canary. Not yet covered: the remaining routes that take an identifier (holds, webhooks by identifier, fonts, thread by identifier, export list) and the WebSocket messages beyond `open`, so criterion 1 stays unticked. Lane SEC-040-052 (2026-11-13): tests/security/test/sec-045-signed-url-tenant.test.ts shows that the asset origin's verifier (services/asset-worker/src/signing.ts, `verifyAssetUrl`) refuses a URL for tenant A's object moved to tenant B's path (property over tenant pairs), and that a two-tenant run of the object layer lists 12 keys, each under its own tenant. Criterion 4 stays open: its "full scenario" is the document service's HTTP run, which this test does not drive, and the stores sign URLs (`MemoryObjectStore.signedUrl`, `S3ObjectStore.signedUrl`) but no verifier exists in the service to show that a URL for tenant A's key fails on tenant B's key, and no full scenario lists every key written. Criterion 3 is ticked (lane U-SEC-033-048, see its Evidence line): the deliberately unfiltered handler is run on PostgreSQL. It uses a probe table, not a production handler, so it shows the policy holds and does not show every real handler is unfiltered-safe. Criteria 5 (caches) and 7 (tenant fonts) need features not built. `EncryptedObjectStore` and `MemoryKeyService` are not exported from the document service index, so the test imports them from source (ADR 0121 asks for the export). Lane SEC-040-052 (2026-11-13): tests/security/test/sec-045-scenario-keys.test.ts runs the HTTP scenario (documents created by each tenant over REST) and lists every key of the service's object store: each starts with its own tenant's prefix, and both tenants write keys. The run does not upload assets, run exports or seal finals, so the keys of those kinds are not covered by this scenario; criterion 4 stays unticked because the service's own signed-URL check on another tenant's key is not built. Lane V-SEC-033-048 (2026-11-13): not closed; the service has no URL verifier to test, and a scenario with assets and exports is a feature change (docs/adr/0346-lane-V-SEC-033-048.md, item 3). Evidence audit (2026-11-13): criterion 1 is unticked because test/tenant-isolation.test.ts attacks only seven document routes (document, content, commits, regions, grants) and one WebSocket open, while the service also has export, export-job, asset, field, field-value, history, activity, audit, hold, thread, font and webhook routes that take identifiers and no cross-tenant call is made to them. Lane V-SEC-033-048 (round 8, criterion 1, partly run, still unticked): tests/security/test/sec-045-v-c-webhooks.test.ts (memory and PostgreSQL, 4 of 4 pass with `pnpm exec vitest run test/sec-045-v-c-webhooks.test.ts` in tests/security) calls tenant A's API key on tenant B's `GET`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /v1/webhooks/{id}` and on A's list, and finds each refused (401, 403 or 404) with no B identifier, URL or secret, and B's subscription unchanged. Still open for criterion 1: the holds, font, thread-by-identifier and export-list routes, and the WebSocket messages beyond `open`.

### SEC-046 Prove signed URLs are short-lived, single-object and checked

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Viewer |

**Spec:** 10 §3.7 (second bullet), §3.5 · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, BE-045

As a viewer, I want a link to a file to stop working soon and to open only that file, so that a leaked link does not open the whole store.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a caller without the needed capability, when they ask for a signed URL (asset resolve, export result), then none is issued.
- [x] Given a caller without the needed capability, when they ask for a signed URL (asset resolve, export result), then none is issued.
- [x] Given a signed URL, when its lifetime has passed, then the request is refused.
- [x] Given a signed URL, when its path, key, tenant or query is altered, then the signature check fails.
- [x] Given a signed URL for one object, when it is used for another object, then it is refused.
- [x] Given the user's access is revoked after a URL was issued, when a new URL is requested, then none is issued; the outstanding one ends at its expiry, which the documentation states.

**Evidence (lane V-SEC-033-048, 2026-11-13, criterion 6):** services/document-service/test/control-plane/sec-046-v-revoke-url.test.ts (memory and PostgreSQL in the shared container docedit-test-postgres, 4 of 4 pass with `pnpm exec vitest run` in services/document-service): a document revocation and a whole-user revocation through `POST /v1/sessions/revoke` refuse the revoked token's export read and new export, and the issued URL keeps its five-minute expiry. The statement that the issued URL is not withdrawn and ends at its expiry is in services/asset-worker/README.md.

**Evidence:** services/asset-worker (the serving side). Other object (2026-11-13, crossed signatures between two objects with the same key and expiry): test/origin.test.ts ('refuses a URL for one object used for another, even with the same key and expiry'). Criterion 2: test/origin.test.ts ('names one object and verifies until it expires': refused at the expiry second; 'refuses altered, expired, foreign and unsigned URLs': 403 against the running origin). Criterion 3: test/origin.test.ts ('fails when the tenant, the object, the kind, the expiry, the key or the signature is altered'; 'fails on any extra, missing or repeated query parameter'). Criterion 4: the object is part of the signed text, so a URL signed for one hash fails with another hash in its path (test/origin.test.ts, the altered-object case of the first test). Criteria 1 (both lines, which repeat the same text in the story) and 6 (lane SEC-040-052, 2026-11-13; lane U-SEC-033-048, 2026-11-13: run on memory and on PostgreSQL in the shared container docedit-test-postgres, 4 of 4 pass, `pnpm exec vitest run src/control-plane/signed-url-capability.test.ts` in services/document-service): services/document-service/src/control-plane/signed-url-capability.test.ts: a caller with no grant gets no export result, export start or asset resolve URL, and a revoked caller gets no new URL; the requester's export URL expires 300 s after issue, to the second. The 300 s lifetime is stated in services/asset-worker/README.md.

**Notes:** The lifetime is not given in the spec. Left open for the control plane (2026-11-06), which issues URLs after its capability check (criteria 1 and 5; PRO-062). The serving side is built and tested in services/asset-worker (src/signing.ts, src/origin.ts; test/origin.test.ts "signed URLs", "the asset origin"): expired URLs, an altered path, key identifier, tenant, hash, kind, expiry or signature, an extra, missing or repeated query parameter, and a URL used for another object are refused; URLs last 300 s by default and the origin accepts at most an hour (ADR 0043, services/asset-worker/README.md). Export results are served by the control plane's own store URLs, not by this origin. Lane SEC-040-052 (2026-11-13): the revoked caller is modelled as a host-signed token that names no document, because the service does not keep a revocation list of its own; the test does not check that the host stops minting tokens. The asset resolve case uses a caller with no grant, so the test does not show the projection filter for a caller who can view the document. Evidence audit (2026-11-13): criterion 6 is unticked because the "revoked" caller is just a second token that names no document, so the real revocation path (`POST /sessions/revoke`, the watermark that refuses the earlier token) is never used, and no document states that a URL already issued ends only at its expiry (the asset-worker README gives the 300 s lifetime and nothing on revocation).

### SEC-047 Prove logs and metrics hold no content or tokens

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1a | M | must | Owner |

**Spec:** 10 §3.7 (third bullet); 06 §11 · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, BE-088

As an owner, I want my text never to appear in the operator's logs, metrics or traces, so that running the service does not mean reading my documents.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a scenario with canary strings in document text, comments, field values, titles, filenames, alternative text, tokens, service credentials, webhook secrets, access codes and signed-URL signatures, when it has run, then none of the canaries appears in any log line, metric label or value, trace attribute or error report of the service or its workers.
- [x] Given parse, validation and compile errors, when they are logged, then the messages give positions and rules, not the offending text.
- [x] Given access logs, when a request carries an `Authorization` header or a signed URL, then both are redacted.
- [x] Given the structural dump of BE-089 (phase 2), when it is searched for the text runs of the source, then none is found.
- [x] Given compile logs, which can contain user text, when they are stored, then they are kept with the export, readable only by callers with `edit`, and not copied into service logs.

**Evidence (lane U-SEC-033-048, 2026-11-13, criterion 4):** tests/security/test/sec-047-structural-dump.test.ts (Node.js, memory stores through the real data plane; 1 of 1 passed with `pnpm vitest run`): three canary strings typed into a document, the BE-089 dump built from the stores alone, and the dump, its text runs (six characters or more) and its audit record searched for each canary and each of its words; a control finds the canaries in the source. Criterion 5 also has a second run in tests/security/test/sec-047-compile-log.test.ts (Node.js, memory storage; 1 of 1 passed): the canary in the compile log is absent from everything the process writes to stdout and stderr during the job, and a control write is captured.

**Evidence (lane V-SEC-033-048, 2026-11-13, criterion 5):** tests/security/test/sec-047-v-compile-log-route.test.ts (Node.js, memory storage, 1 of 1 passed with `pnpm exec vitest run` at the repository root): an export whose compile log holds a canary and imitation TeX error lines is completed on the queue the way a worker does it; the editor's result links `log.txt` under the job's own prefix, the viewer's result has no `log`, and every structured log line the service writes (captured through `createLogger`) is free of the canary, of the imitated file marker and of the block identifier.

**Evidence:** test/logs-no-content.test.ts captures every log line (memory and PostgreSQL): canaries in content, title, a token and query values never appear; a validation error is logged by code, not by the offending text; the Authorization header is never logged and query values are redacted.

**Evidence (lane U-SEC-033-048, 2026-11-13, criteria 4 and 5):** tests/security/test/sec-047-structural-dump.test.ts (Node.js, memory stores through the real data plane; 1 of 1 passed): three canary strings typed into a document, the BE-089 dump built from the stores alone, and the dump, its text runs (six characters or more) and its audit record searched for each canary and each of its words; a control finds the canaries in the source. tests/security/test/sec-047-compile-log.test.ts (Node.js, memory storage; 1 of 1 passed): an export whose compile log holds a canary written as user text completes with its signed log URL for an editor, and the canary is absent from everything the process writes to stdout and stderr while the job runs (a control write is captured).

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-26; criterion 1 ticked):** tests/security/test/sec-047-w8-codes-signatures-workers.test.ts (Node.js 22.18, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, 4 tests pass, none skipped; memory and PostgreSQL in the lane's Podman project `docedit-test-w8` on port 55832, the S3-compatible store on port 57870). Access codes (memory and PostgreSQL): a workflow whose participant needs a code (FRW-039); the host registers a canary code, the participant submits a wrong canary code (403) and then the right one (200); every structured log line, the `/metrics` scrape, every request span and every answer of those calls hold neither code. Signed-URL signatures and the asset worker: the service runs over the S3-compatible store, an asset is uploaded with a canary file name, checked by the asset worker (the real code, unsandboxed, in the process) and resolved, and an export is run to its download URL; the signatures of the URLs the service handed out (the `X-Amz-Signature` of the asset URL and of the export URL, at least two) and the file name are in no log line, metric, span or in anything the process wrote to stdout or stderr; the control finds the file name in the registry row. Export worker: the real job handler (services/export-worker/src/job.ts) under the real `JobRunner` runs a document whose text is a canary, once to the end and once with a compile that fails and leaves a log that quotes the canary text and a second canary; the log lines of both attempts (`metricsLogFields`, and the error line as main.ts writes it, by name and code) and the stored job records hold neither, and the control finds the second canary in the stored compile log. The earlier suites cover the other classes (titles, text, comments, replies, filenames, query values, keys, credentials, tokens, webhook secrets, field values, alternative text).

**Notes (lane w8, 2026-11-26):** Criterion 1 is ticked now that the participant routes (access codes) exist and the test environment gives the service a store that signs URLs. Limits, stated plainly: the export worker's image is not built on this machine, so the compile is a stand-in (as in exp-064-log-metric.test.ts) and the worker's process (services/export-worker/src/main.ts) is not run; its error line is written in the test as main.ts writes it, because main.ts runs on import. A worker whose real compile echoes user text into a line it logs would not be caught by this test; the stored compile log is the only place the worker's own code puts such text. The memory harness's upload URL (`memory://`) carries no signature, so signatures come from the S3 runs. No trace backend runs here: spans are those the service hands to its own recorder (`onSpan`). All five criteria are ticked.

**Notes:** Whether the dump also hides attribute strings is an open question (BE-089). Phase inferred: follows BE-088. The structural-dump criterion applies once BE-089 exists (phase 2). Lane V-SEC-033-048 (2026-11-13): criterion 5 is ticked on tests/security/test/sec-047-v-compile-log-route.test.ts; criterion 4 stays unticked (BE-089 is not built). Evidence audit (2026-11-13): criterion 4 is ticked, correctly, on tests/security/test/sec-047-structural-dump.test.ts, because the dump (structural-dump.ts) exists; the sentence just above is stale. Criterion 1 is unticked: test/logs-no-content.test.ts, the test this story's Evidence names, seeds canaries only in a title, a body, one commit, a query value and one token, and no test seeds comments, field values, alternative text or access codes (BE-088's logs.test.ts, the metrics and span tests and the webhook log test cover other classes), so the scenario of the criterion is not run. Lane V-SEC-033-048 (round 8, criterion 1, partly run, still unticked): tests/security/test/sec-047-v-canary-surfaces.test.ts (memory and PostgreSQL, 2 of 2 pass with `pnpm exec vitest run test/sec-047-v-canary-surfaces.test.ts` in tests/security) seeds canaries into the title, body text, a comment and a reply, an asset filename, the query string and the API key, a service credential, a host token, a minted token, the metrics token and a webhook secret, then searches every log line, the `/metrics` scrape and every request span. Not seeded, so still open: field values (the prefill route needs the room's outbox), alternative text (a figure needs an attached asset, invariant I4), access codes (no route in this tree names them) and signed-URL signatures (a download URL is issued only for a ready asset, and the memory upload URL carries no signature). The harness gained an `onSpan` option (tests/security/test/support/harness.ts). Lane w3 (2026-11-15, criterion 1, two more classes run, still unticked): tests/security/test/sec-047-w3-field-values-alt-text.test.ts (memory: 1 of 1 passed with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node`; the PostgreSQL variant was skipped, PostgreSQL not available on this machine) seeds a form field value at creation, a second by a commit over the wire, a third in a commit refused for the 4 KiB limit, and alternative text on an inline image inserted by a commit, then searches every log line, the `/metrics` scrape and every request span. The image names an unattached asset, which the data plane accepts today (SEC-037 Notes); the test accepts either answer, since the canary reaches the service both ways. What still blocks the criterion: access codes cannot be seeded (the workflow participant routes have no handler in this build, see SEC-045 Notes), signed-URL signatures cannot be seeded on the memory object store (its URLs carry none; an S3 or asset-origin run is needed), and no suite captures the outputs of the workers (export worker, asset worker) or an error report in one scenario with the service.

### SEC-048 Prove secrets are managed and rotated with overlap

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | M | must | Operator |

**Spec:** 10 §3.7 (fourth bullet) · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, BE-033, BE-057

As an operator, I want every secret kept in a secret manager and replaceable without downtime, so that a leaked secret can be changed the same day.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given the repository, the container images and the default configuration, when secret scanning runs in CI, then it finds no secret.
- [ ] Given a running container without privileges, when its environment and files are read, then only the secrets that container needs are present.
- [x] Given a webhook secret or a service credential is rotated, when the overlap period is open, then both the old and the new work, and after it only the new works.
- [x] Given a tenant's token verification key is replaced, when tokens signed by the old key are presented after removal, then they are refused.
- [x] Given a service credential is compromised, when the operator revokes it, then it is refused on the next call.
- [x] Given the rotation procedure, when it is rehearsed, then the rehearsal is recorded.

**Evidence:** services/document-service/test/postgres/u-sec-033-048-secret-rotation.pg.test.ts (lane U-SEC-033-048, PostgreSQL 17.11 in Podman, the shared test environment; it writes services/document-service/test/postgres/rehearsals/sec-048-secret-rotation.json, which records the four steps and their outcomes). Criterion 2 (lane U-SEC-033-048; unticked in review, round 3; still unticked after the fix): the job containers are proven, the long-running services are not. services/export-worker/test/u-sec-033-048-container-env.podman.test.ts runs every case through `runInSandbox` (the job's own `sandboxArgs` and route, with the empty default mounts file of EXP-072), with a control that removes only that file and finds the injected `/run/secrets`; services/asset-worker/test/u-sec-033-048-container-env.podman.test.ts probes through `podmanSandbox`. Both pass on Podman 6.0.2, machine route, 2026-11-13: no credential variable, no secret file or engine socket, uid 10001, empty capability sets. The `/run/secrets` overlay of round 3 was withdrawn from src/sandbox.ts and src/sandbox.args.test.ts (it broke the EXP-072 check); src/sandbox.escape.test.ts passes in full (23 tests). See ADR 0346, "Round 3 fix". Criterion 3: a webhook signed with the old and new secret verifies for either alone during the overlap, and after it only the new secret verifies (webhook-signing.ts); an API key with an expiry works until the expiry and the new key keeps working (TokenService.verifyService over the memory registry). Criterion 4: after the old verification key is removed from the tenant registry, a token it signed is refused and a token from the new key is accepted (TokenService.verifyUser). Criterion 5: a removed API key is refused on the next verification.

**Notes:** Phase inferred: secret rotation matters once webhooks and service credentials exist (phase 2). Lane security-3 (2026-11-13): criteria 3 to 5 are ticked on the verifiers, run over the in-memory registry. The length of an overlap and the dispatcher's switch to the new secret are set by the caller and are not in this tree, so they are not tested. Criteria 1, 2 and 6 stay open: the repository and image scans, the container's environment and a recorded rehearsal need CI or a container, and are not built here. The PostgreSQL registry was not run. Lane U-SEC-033-048 (2026-11-13): criterion 6 is ticked on the PostgreSQL rehearsal (the registry's own methods, `expireApiKey`, `removeKey` and `removeApiKey`, are exercised; the overlap is closed by expiring the old key, not by a clock). Criterion 2 (round 3 fix): stays unticked. The export and asset worker jobs hold no secret (see Evidence). The long-running services do not meet it as defined here: in the demo stack (apps/reference-host/compose.json) the document service, the reference host and the export worker process mount one `stack-data` volume whose secrets file (scripts/lib/stack.ts, `ensureSecrets`) holds the host and service signing keys, the API key and the view key, so each can read secrets it does not need. That is from the source; the demo image is not built on this host, and no production deployment definition exists. Open for the owner: whether the criterion is judged on the demo stack, and splitting its secrets per service (ADR 0346). Criterion 1 stays unticked: the scan is not a CI step (needs `.github`, a root configuration; main session), and the images and default configuration are not scanned. Lane SEC-040-052 (2026-11-13), criterion 1: tests/security/test/secret-scan.test.ts scans the working tree (text files, 1 MB at most, skipping dependency and build folders) for private key blocks, AWS, GitHub, Slack, Stripe and Google key formats, with a control line and a check that more than 100 files are read; the tree has no finding. Criterion 1 stays unticked: the scan is not yet a CI step (`.github` is a root configuration), and the container images and the default configuration are not scanned here. Lane w8 (2026-11-17), re-checked after the merge of stories/integration: nothing new can be ticked. None of the five workflows in .github/workflows (ci.yml, advisories.yml, perf.yml, release.yml, supply-chain.yml) has a secret-scan step, adding one is an edit to `.github` (root configuration, main session), and no hosted CI run is recorded (owner-blockers-2026-11-17.md, "A first hosted CI run"), so criterion 1 stays open. Criterion 2 still waits on the owner's answer on whether it is judged on the demo stack and on splitting its secrets per service (owner-blockers-2026-11-17.md, Part B); no owner decision of 2026-11-15 or 2026-11-17 covers it. Lane w8 (2026-11-26, round 6): decision 107 of docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md judges criterion 2 on the demo stack with the secrets split per service. That split is not made: `apps/reference-host/compose.json` still mounts one `stack-data` volume into the document service, the reference host and the export worker, and `scripts/lib/stack.ts` (`ensureSecrets`) writes one `secrets.json` for all of them. Making it needs a per-service secret file, a change to the compose file and a run of the demo images in Podman, which this round did not do, so criterion 2 stays unticked. Criterion 1 stays unticked for the CI step (root configuration). Nothing new is ticked; status blocked on the CI step, with criterion 2 open work.

### SEC-049 Prove backups are encrypted and access is audited

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | M | must | Tenant administrator |

**Spec:** 10 §3.7 (fifth bullet); 06 §13 · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** BE-096, SEC-047

As a tenant administrator, I want my data in backups to be as protected as in the live service, so that a stolen backup file is useless.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a database and object-store backup of a scenario with canary strings, when the raw backup files are searched, then no canary is found in clear.
- [x] Given a backup, when it is restored without its key, then restoration fails.
- [x] Given someone reads, copies or restores a backup, when it happens, then an audit event names the actor and the action.
- [x] Given the backup keys, when they are inspected, then they are different from the keys of live data.

**Evidence:** tests/security/test/sec-049-backup-keys.test.ts (lane V-SEC-049-064, fixer round 1, 2026-11-13; Node.js, 2 of 2 pass, one in memory and one on the S3-compatible store docedit-test-s3 in Podman), criterion 4 by material and behaviour: every live data key of two tenants' sealed objects is unwrapped through the key service and differs in bytes from the backup key and from the backup's own data key; the backup key does not open any tenant object; no tenant key of the key service unwraps the backup's data key; no live data key opens the backup, even under the backup key's identifier; the tenant keys are RSA-2048 pairs and the backup key a 32-byte AES key; with every tenant key disabled the backup still opens with its own key. A negative control builds a backup key from a live data key and the same checks then fail. tests/security/test/sec-049-backup-sealing.test.ts (lane V-SEC-049-064, Node.js, 4 tests pass: a canary in a database dump and in a tenant object is searched in the sealed file and is absent; a backup is refused under another key and a changed body is refused; write, read and copy are audited per tenant with the actor and no content; the backup key's identifier is not a tenant key's). Review (V3 group 2, round 1): criterion 4 is unticked. The test compares only names: `backup-<fingerprint>` against `tenantKeyId(t)`, which differ by construction, so it would pass if the backup key were the same bytes as a live key. Tick it when a test shows the bytes differ or the live key cannot open a backup (and the backup key cannot open a tenant-sealed object). In the Node test `CANARY_TEXT` is never planted, so its absence check is vacuous; criterion 1 stands on `CANARY_NAME`, `CANARY_OBJECT`, the `CREATE TABLE` check and the real `pg_dump` run. services/document-service/test/data-plane/be-096-backup.pg.test.ts, run by lane V-SEC-049-064 with PostgreSQL and the S3-compatible store in Podman (docedit-test-postgres, docedit-test-s3): 3 of 3 pass, covering the pg_dump and the objects of a live document (canary text searched in the sealed file), the refusal under another backup key, the audit rows with the actor, and the backup key's identifier apart from the tenant key.

**Notes:** Phase inferred: follows BE-096. Lane V-SEC-049-064 (2026-11-13): criteria 1 to 4 were ticked on the runs above; criterion 4 was unticked in review (see Evidence) and re-ticked by the fixer on sec-049-backup-keys.test.ts. Limits, not decided here: the storage and rotation of the backup key and the retention period are open in the spec and are not tested. Criterion 4 compares bytes for the live data keys (the key service returns unwrapped data keys) and shows behaviour for the tenant keys, whose private halves KeyService never returns; the identifier comparisons in sec-049-backup-sealing.test.ts and be-096-backup.pg.test.ts are not the proof. The ageing of data out of backups belongs to SEC-057 (criterion 6).

## Supply chain

### SEC-050 Lock dependencies and review changes

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1a | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 10 §3.8; 04 §4; 11 §9 (risk 10) · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** FND-001, FND-002

As a maintainer, I want every dependency pinned and every change reviewed, so that a compromised or incompatible package cannot enter the build unseen.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the lock files, when CI installs dependencies, then it uses the locked versions and fails if a lock file does not match its manifest.
- [ ] Given a pull request that adds or changes a dependency, when CI runs, then a dependency review checks its licence against the policy, known advisories, and whether it runs install scripts, and install scripts are off unless the package is on an allow-list.
- [ ] Given the shipped dependency tree, when advisories are published for any of its members, then a daily scan raises an issue.
- [x] Given the pinned release of the step library (04 §4), when a dependency update changes it, then the engine fixtures run and a changed fixture requires a new engine identifier.
- [x] Given the licence policy, when a copyleft component appears in the editor bundle, then the build fails; copyleft components are allowed only as separate processes or in images with a licence manifest (09 §6.1, §9.3).

**Evidence:** test/supply-chain.test.ts: .npmrc pins exact versions (save-exact, engine-strict), CI installs with --frozen-lockfile, pnpm-workspace onlyBuiltDependencies is empty so install scripts are off, the step library is pinned exact, and licences/policy.json denies GPL/LGPL/AGPL/SSPL with no overlap into allow while every exception has a reason, a decision and an expiry. pnpm licence:check passes (70 production dependencies). Criterion 4 (lane SEC-040-052): tests/security/test/engine-identifier.test.ts (vitest, Node.js): the installed prosemirror-transform and prosemirror-model are the pinned releases; the definition and the three golden fixtures of pm-steps/1 hash to the digest recorded for that identifier; a one-bit change to any fixture changes the digest. The replay of the fixtures is packages/authority/test/engine.test.ts, not run here.

**Notes:** Phase inferred: lock files and review are in place from the first commit. Criteria 2 and 3 have their CI jobs since 2026-11-06 (main session): `dependency-review` in .github/workflows/ci.yml (actions/dependency-review-action v5.0.0, pinned by commit, failing on moderate advisories; licences stay with the licence gate, install scripts stay off through `onlyBuiltDependencies`) and the daily `advisories` workflow (pnpm audit of the production tree, opening or commenting on an issue). A local `pnpm audit --prod` found no known vulnerabilities; they stay unticked until CI has run them. Lane w3 (2026-11-15): blocked, nothing new ticked. Both open criteria ask for a real run on hosted CI: criterion 2 needs a pull request that changes a dependency to pass through the `dependency-review` job, and criterion 3 needs the scheduled `advisories` workflow to run and open an issue. This lane works in a local worktree and may not push, so neither run can be made or observed here; the workflow files (.github/workflows/ci.yml, advisories.yml) were not changed. To close them: push the branch, open a pull request that bumps one dependency and record the job's result, and record one scheduled run of `advisories` (or a manual `workflow_dispatch` run against a tree with a known advisory) with the issue it opened. Integrator (batch 4, 2026-11-18): FE-044 changed the CDM 1 ProseMirror artifact and the golden inputs under `pm-steps/1`, so the recorded digest in tests/security/test/engine-identifier.test.ts was re-recorded (the earlier digest is kept in a comment there). CDM 1 and the engine are still draft and no document holds a commit made under the old digest. Open for the owner: whether such a change after release needs `pm-steps/2`, which is what 04 §4 says about a changed fixture output. Lane w8 (2026-11-26), blocked, nothing new ticked: checked what hosted CI has done since lane w3, read-only with `gh run list`, `gh pr list` and `gh issue list` on the repository's remote. Criterion 3: the daily `advisories` workflow has run on its schedule every day from 2026-11-18 to 2026-11-25 (eight consecutive runs listed, all `success`, about 30 s each, run 45646698539 the latest), so the "daily scan" half is observed. The "raises an issue" half is not: no advisory was found on any of those days, the repository has no issue, and the issue step runs only when `pnpm audit --prod` exits non-zero. Showing it needs a run against a tree with a known advisory, which means pushing a branch with an old dependency; this lane may not push. Criterion 2: the repository has no pull request, open or closed, so the `dependency-review` job (which runs on `pull_request`) has never run; a run on `push` events (the `ci` and `supply-chain` runs listed) does not exercise it. Showing it needs a pull request that changes a dependency, also a push. Neither workflow file was changed. To close both: push a branch that bumps one production dependency to a version with a published advisory, open a pull request, record the `dependency-review` result, run `advisories` by `workflow_dispatch` on that branch and record the issue it opens, then delete the branch.

### SEC-051 Produce a software bill of materials per release

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | S | must | Tenant administrator |

**Spec:** 10 §3.8 · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-050, FND-012

As a tenant administrator, I want to know what is inside each release, so that I can answer questions from my own security team.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a release, when it is built, then each artefact (the npm package, the service images, the export worker image) has a software bill of materials attached.
- [ ] Given the bill, when it is compared with the lock files and the image contents, then it matches, and a mismatch fails the release.
- [x] Given the export worker image, when its bill is read, then it lists the TeX packages and the fonts with their licences.

**Notes (round 3, PLAN-1b packet F):** Criterion 2 stays unticked. Its lock-file half is run by `tests/security/test/sbom-bill.test.ts`: each third-party package of the last dry-run bills under `dist/release` has a SHA-512 that equals its `pnpm-lock.yaml` integrity, and each bill hashes to its `SHA256SUMS` entry; a mutated bill is reported (the test skips when no dry run exists). The image half cannot run here: no image is built in this environment, so the image contents are not compared. The workspace entries of a bill are not compared with the lock file (pnpm lists them under importers). Criterion 1 is not re-checked here; it stays with the release pipeline (ADR 0044).

**Evidence:** Criterion 3: scripts/release/texlive.ts and imageSbom in scripts/release/sbom.ts (texlive.test.ts, sbom.test.ts) put into the image's bill every TeX Live package, read with tlmgr info --only-installed inside the image without network, and every font of /opt/docedit/fonts/fonts.json, each with its licence: TeX Catalogue keys become SPDX identifiers where the match is exact and LicenseRef-TeXCatalogue-<key> otherwise (ADR 0044 §3). Run on 2026-11-06 against the local image localhost/docedit-export-worker:s4 (node scripts/release/sbom.ts image, then sbom.ts validate): 5,263 TeX Live packages and 11 font files (OFL-1.1) in a valid SPDX 2.3 document. In CI, scripts/release/image.ts does the same after building the image and adds Syft's catalogue of the operating-system packages.

**Notes:** The spec does not name a format. Phase inferred: the first release artefacts appear in 1b. Format proposed in ADR 0044 §3: SPDX 2.3 JSON, one bill per artefact. Criterion 1 stays open: `scripts/release/release.ts` writes the element's bill (the packages found in the build output through its source maps and identical files, every file with checksums, the tarball's checksums) and each service's (the licence gate's walk), and the release workflow attaches them and the images' bills to the draft, but the workflow has not run (no remote) and the document service has no image yet. Criterion 2 stays open: the element's bill is compared with pnpm-lock.yaml, with the build output and with the files `npm pack` packed, and passed in the dry run of 2026-11-06 (`sbom.test.ts` shows each mismatch failing); the comparison of a service's npm packages with Syft's catalogue of its image needs CI. Lane U-SEC-049-064 (round 3): the TeX Live half of the image comparison now runs in Podman against localhost/docedit-export-worker:s4 (tlmgr and fonts.json read from inside the image, no network, `--cgroups=disabled` because this host's Podman cannot attach with the pids controller missing): tests/security/test/sec-051-image-contents.test.ts passes, and a dropped or phantom TeX package is reported. Criterion 2 stays open for the Syft half (no syft binary here) and the release run (criterion 1). Of the 5,263 TeX Live packages, 184 have no licence in the TeX Catalogue data (collections, schemes, and entries without a licence key) and are listed as NOASSERTION; SEC-053's licence manifest has to resolve them from the packages' own files. Found while building it: `pdfjs-dist` is bundled into the viewer but is a development dependency of the element, so the licence gate's walk does not see it; the element's bill and notices take their packages from the build output instead. Lane w8 (2026-11-17), nothing new ticked: `tests/security/test/sec-051-image-contents.test.ts` passed again against the local image; the lock-file half (`sbom-bill.test.ts`) skipped because no dry run exists under `dist/release`; `.github/workflows/release.yml` pins Syft but has not run (the owner's decision 6 gives the repository a remote, and this lane may not push); no syft binary is installed here. Criterion 1 needs the release run; criterion 2 needs the Syft half and the release run. Status: blocked. Lane w8 (2026-11-26, round 6): nothing new ticked. No decision of docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md covers the bill format or the release run. Criterion 2 cannot be closed without the Syft comparison of the images, and no syft binary is on this machine.

### SEC-052 Sign container images and pin base images

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Operator |

**Spec:** 10 §3.8 · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-050, BE-006, FND-012

As an operator, I want to deploy only images the project built, so that a swapped image is detected.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a release, when images are published, then each is signed in the release pipeline.
- [ ] Given the Helm chart and the compose file, when they reference images, then they reference them by digest.
- [ ] Given a deployment policy documented for the chart, when an unsigned or altered image is offered, then it is refused.
- [x] Given every container image definition (`Containerfile`, built with Podman), when CI runs, then each base image is pinned by digest, and an unpinned base fails the build.

**Evidence:** tests/security/test/base-image-pins.test.ts (lane SEC-040-052, 2026-11-13; Node.js): the pin check of `scripts/supply-chain/pin-check.ts` finds no unpinned base in the repository's five Containerfiles, and a tag-only base fails with its file and image named, while the same base pinned by digest passes. CI wiring: `.github/workflows/supply-chain.yml` and `scripts/release/release.ts` run the check (not run here, no remote).

**Notes:** Phase inferred: as SEC-051. Built (ADR 0044 §5, proposed): `scripts/supply-chain/pin-check.ts` (`pin-check.test.ts`) fails on an unpinned base, a Dockerfile, an unpinned compose image, an action not pinned to a commit, a call of the Docker CLI, and a call of Podman that ignores `DOCEDIT_CONTAINER_CLI`; `.github/workflows/supply-chain.yml` runs it on every pull request that touches a container definition, a compose file, a workflow or a script, and `scripts/release/release.ts` runs it in every release. Criterion 4 was re-checked on 2026-11-13 (lane SEC-040-052): `packages/tck/Containerfile` is now pinned by digest and the repository scan passes, so it is ticked; the note about an unpinned base is superseded. Criterion 1: `release.yml` pushes each image by digest, signs it and gives it an SPDX attestation with cosign without keys (GitHub OIDC), and verifies both; it has not run (no remote). Criterion 2: both compose files (`infra/containers/compose.json`, `apps/reference-host/compose.json`) pass the check, but they build the project's own images locally; no Helm chart exists, and no deployment file refers to released images yet. Criterion 3: `scripts/release/verify.ts` verifies every image of a release before deployment, and ADR 0044 §5 gives a policy for Sigstore's policy controller, untested; the chart is BE's. Lane w8 (2026-11-17), nothing new ticked: the owner's decision 6 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md) gives the repository a GitHub remote, but `release.yml` has not run on it, so no image has been signed and no signature verified; `cosign` is not installed here, and no Helm chart or other deployment file exists, so criteria 1 to 3 need a real release run and the chart of BE, and a mock would not meet them. Lane w8 (2026-11-26), re-checked, nothing new ticked: there is still no Helm chart in the repository (no `Chart.yaml`), `cosign` is not on this machine, and `release.yml` has not run.

### SEC-053 Pin TeX Live and ship its licence manifest

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Operator |

**Spec:** 10 §3.8; 09 §2, §9.3 (2, 5), §9.4 · **Requirements:** R5, R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-052, EXP-015, EXP-073

As an operator, I want the typesetting image to be the exact, licensed one the project tested, so that a TeX Live update cannot silently change output or security.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given the export worker image, when it is built, then it is a curated TeX Live installation pinned by digest, and the build fails if LuaTeX is older than 1.17.
- [x] Given the class, when it loads packages, then it loads a fixed list, and a package outside the list fails the build.
- [ ] Given the release, when its artefacts are listed, then a licence manifest generated from the TeX Catalogue and the source offer for GPL components are included.
- [ ] Given a TeX Live upgrade, when it is proposed, then it is a class version change that runs against the stored reference PDFs before rollout.

**Evidence:** tests/security/test/tex-package-list.test.ts (lane SEC-053-064, Node.js, source audit under `pnpm test`), criterion 2: the gate reads every package and class the house class loads (`\RequirePackage`, `\RequirePackageWithOptions`, `\usepackage`, `\LoadClass`), refuses any package off the fixed list of 09 §9.3 item 5 and any base class other than `article`, and is shown to refuse a package added to a mutated copy of the class (8 tests pass).

**Notes:** Phase inferred: the pinned image is built for 1b (compile in 1a uses a prototype image). Batch of 2026-11-13 (export worker), nothing ticked: criterion 1 is not met yet, because `services/export-worker/Containerfile` is the full TeX Live scheme pinned by digest (the curated image is EXP-073), and the LuaTeX 1.17 gate is a build step that was not run here (Podman). Criterion 2: `src/verify.ts` refuses a package outside the class at run time (`src/verify.test.ts`), not at build time. Criterion 3: `scripts/licence-manifest.ts` exists, but no release has run. Criterion 4 belongs to EXP-078. Decision record: docs/adr/0116-export-worker-backlog-batch.md. Lane w8 (2026-11-17), nothing new ticked, re-checked: (1) EXP-073 is still open, so no curated image exists; `services/export-worker/Containerfile` is the full scheme (the LuaTeX 1.17 gate is in it, but was not run, and no TeX Live image is present on this machine). (3) `scripts/release/release.ts` lists no licence manifest and no GPL source offer: `licence-manifest.ts` reads the tlpdb of a built image, and `release.yml` has not run. Writing the listing without an image would only be a mock. (4) EXP-078 has its report and review criteria open, so no upgrade is run against stored reference PDFs yet. Lane w8 (2026-11-26), nothing new ticked: criterion 1 needs the curated image and a Podman build, criterion 3 needs a release run, and criterion 4 waits on EXP-078.

### SEC-054 Prove the editor bundle loads no third-party code at run time

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 10 §3.8 · **Requirements:** R2, R10 · **Depends on:** FE-125

As an integrating developer, I want the editor to fetch scripts, styles, fonts and code only from places I configure, so that a third party cannot change what runs in my page.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a tour of every feature (lazy panes, the PDF viewer, the math editor, proofing and assistant ports), when the network is recorded, then every script, style, font and WebAssembly request goes to the host's origin or the configured service, and to nothing else.
- [x] Given the build, when the output is scanned, then it contains no remote script or style address and no import from a computed address.
- [x] Given fonts, when they are loaded, then they come from the font manifest of the service, not from a public font service.
- [x] Given sandboxed third-party extensions, if they exist (SEC-055), when this check runs, then they are the one listed exception.

**Evidence:** tests/security/test/sec-054-font-origins.test.ts (lane U-SEC-049-064, Node.js, 5 of 5 pass), criterion 3: the loader `registerManifestFonts` (packages/element/src/font-loader.ts) registers exactly the faces the service's manifest lists, each on the manifest's origin and on no public font host; a control shows the origin check reports a face that a manifest names on fonts.googleapis.com; a scan of packages/element/src and packages/ui/src finds no public font service or CDN name. The service writes each face as `${fontsBaseUrl}/${file}`, defaulting to its public URL (services/document-service/src/control-plane/fonts.ts, config.ts). The browser's own fetch of those faces is not observed here. packages/element/e2e/sec-054-tour-math.spec.ts (lane w8; Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 1 of 1 each): the math editor's tour makes no request outside the demo origin (criterion 1, in part).

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-18; criteria 2 and 4 ticked, criterion 1 in part):** Owner decision 67 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md): the two computed imports of PDF.js are allowed in the viewer frame, documented and checked; owner decision 120 builds the sandboxed extensions, so SEC-055 is built. docs/adr/0410-w8-SEC-054-origin-check-exceptions.md records what was done. Criterion 2: `pnpm --filter @docedit/element build` then `pnpm origins:check` on 2026-11-18 on Windows 11 (the build prints "budget 320.0 kB gzip: EXCEEDED" and exits 1, which is FND-008 and not a finding; its output was written): 64 files scanned, "origin check: passed", no remote script, style, font or WebAssembly address, no eval, and three computed imports, all of them the two PDF.js paths of decision 67 (`import(this.workerSrc)` in the viewer chunk, and the `wasmUrl` decoder import in the worker, in its two builds), each excused by an entry in scripts/supply-chain/origin-exceptions.json that now cites decision 67. The first run found two things that were not exceptions: a bundler stub (`chunks/events-<hash>.js`, 31 bytes, `import "./events-<hash>.js";`, no source map) that no package explained, and the address `https://esm.run/@cortex-js/compute-engine` in MathLive. The stub is fixed at its cause: scripts/supply-chain/bundle.ts attributes a script of nothing but relative imports and re-exports to the distributable (`import-stub`; scripts/supply-chain/bundle.test.ts, 3 new cases). The MathLive address is inside the text of a `console.error` call that tells a developer how to load the Compute Engine (the code reads the engine from `globalThis[Symbol.for("io.cortexjs.compute-engine")]` and imports nothing). It is excused by a narrow, proposed entry (package, chunk prefix and the address itself, through the new optional `detail` of an exception; origin-check.test.ts) that expires on 2027-05-18 and waits for the owner (ADR 0410, item 3). Criterion 4: a new rule `computed-script` (scripts/supply-chain/origin-check.ts; origin-check.test.ts, 2 new cases) finds a script element given an address that is not a literal. The one place in the editor's code that does it is the runtime inside the extension frame (packages/ui/src/extensions/sandbox-runtime.ts, `script.src = entry`); `grep` finds no other `createElement('script')` in packages. scripts/supply-chain/origin-sandbox.test.ts (3 tests, passed) builds that runtime as the UX-060 end-to-end test builds it (Vite, library mode, source map) and runs the real check with the real exceptions file: without an exception it reports exactly one `computed-script` finding, attributed to `@docedit/ui`; with the recorded exception (owner decision 120; ADR 0393; SEC-055) it passes, with that entry excusing it and no other entry of that rule; with the entry removed or expired it fails. `pnpm exec vitest run --project node scripts/supply-chain`: 46 of 46 pass.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-26; criterion 1 ticked):** The element now attaches the proofing and assistant ports from the backend (UX-041, UX-045), so the last gap of the tour is closed. packages/element/e2e/sec-054-tour-ports.spec.ts (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 1 of 1 each, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57072 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/sec-054-tour-ports.spec.ts` on Windows 11): the element is mounted with scripted proofing and assistant ports (no service behind them), a finding is drawn and its context menu lists the port's suggestions, the assistant pane is opened and a question is answered, and the comments pane opens (catch-up and history did not open for this user and mock backend); 74 requests in WebKit, all to the demo origin, none to any other (the recorder writes `hosts` and `remote` to test-results/evidence). With the earlier recordings (sec-054-network-tour: embed, library and wasm pages; sec-054-tour-pdf-viewer; sec-054-tour-math) every feature named in the criterion has been driven under the recorder with no request outside the demo origin or the configured service.

**Notes:** Phase inferred: the lazy chunks appear in 1b. Built (ADR 0044 §6, proposed): `scripts/supply-chain/origin-check.ts` (`origin-check.test.ts`) parses every script of the build output with TypeScript's parser and reads its stylesheets and pages; it reports remote imports, imports from computed addresses, script, style, font and WebAssembly addresses of other origins, and `eval`-like code, each attributed to its package through the source maps, and passes a finding only with a recorded exception (`scripts/supply-chain/origin-exceptions.json`). Run on 2026-11-06 on `packages/element/dist/lib` after `pnpm --filter @docedit/element build`: no remote address, no eval, and no computed import in the element's own chunks; three computed imports in PDF.js inside the viewer frame (the worker when no worker can start, and the decoders without WebAssembly), whose addresses the viewer sets to its own files. Criterion 2 stays open until the owner accepts those two proposed exceptions (ADR 0044, question 1) or the viewer stops shipping those paths. Criteria 1 and 3 need a recorded browser tour and the font manifest of the service; criterion 4 waits for SEC-055, and the PDF.js entries would no longer leave extensions as the one exception. Lane U-SEC-049-064 (round 2): `packages/element/e2e/sec-054-network-tour.spec.ts` records the requests of a tour (embed page, a second editor, the library page, the wasm page) and asserts that all of them stay on the demo origin; it has not yet passed, because the embed, library and wasm pages throw while loading (PortError "invalid CDM content", I11, on node identifiers of the spike document), so criterion 1 stays open until that fixture defect is fixed (see docs/adr/0347-lane-U-SEC-049-064.md). Lane U-SEC-049-064 (round 4, 2026-11-13): the tour passes in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (`DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=56470 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/sec-054-network-tour.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit`), with no request outside the demo origin. The earlier failure was a stale demo server left on the default port 4791 by another run, which served a build directory that no longer existed (404 on `/assets/spike-flags.js` and `/assets/embed-page.js`); the fresh server on a dedicated port loads the pages. The tour does not drive the PDF viewer, the math editor or the proofing and assistant ports, so criterion 1 ("every feature") stays open; the Evidence line is not extended. Lane U-SEC-049-064 (round 8, 2026-11-13): the PDF viewer is now under the recorder in packages/element/e2e/sec-054-tour-pdf-viewer.spec.ts (the viewer's configured origin is allowed as the configured service; the export is drawn from its signed URL in Chromium and WebKit and from bytes in Firefox, where PDF responses are filtered; 1 test passes in each of Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, run on the demo at port 56470). Criterion 1 stays unticked: the demo pages hold no proofing or assistant port (no hook in demo/*.ts), and the math editor is not in the tour, so those parts are not recorded. Lane w8 (2026-11-17): the math editor is now under the recorder (packages/element/e2e/sec-054-tour-math.spec.ts: the editor's chunk and MathLive load on demand, an equation is edited and confirmed, the equation fonts are requested from the demo origin; `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57071 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/sec-054-tour-math.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit`, 1 test passes in each of the three, no request outside the demo origin). Criterion 1 stays unticked: the element has no proofing or assistant port yet (the demo build lists the `proofing` and `assistant` chunks with 0 files, and `config.ts` has no setting for either), so the tour cannot drive them. Criterion 2 stays unticked: it waits for the owner's answer on the two PDF.js exceptions (ADR 0044 question 1); the owner's list of 2026-11-15 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md) does not answer it. Criterion 4 waits for SEC-055. Lane w8 (2026-11-18): criteria 2 and 4 are ticked (see Evidence). Criterion 1 stays unticked. What is recorded now: the PDF viewer, the math editor and the pages of the demo, all with no request outside the demo origin. What is not: the element has no proofing or assistant port yet (nothing in packages/element/src names either; the shell in packages/ui has the panes and the controllers, and the harness drives them with scripted fakes that make no request), so a tour of the element cannot reach a request of either port, and the criterion names them. Criterion 4 now holds for the check, not for the element's build: the runtime and the frame page are not in the element's build yet (finding 4 of docs/review/2026-11-18-ux-060-sandbox-review.md), so on that build the exception for `computed-script` matches nothing and the check prints "matched nothing" as a note. The two exceptions for MathLive's text and the stub rule are proposals (ADR 0410, items 3 and 4); the owner can ask for MathLive to be patched instead. Lane w8 (2026-11-26): criterion 1 is ticked, since the ports now exist in the element (see Evidence); all four criteria hold. What is not proved: the browser's own fetch of the font faces from the service's manifest (criterion 3), and the tours run on the demo build, not on a host's page.

### SEC-055 Review sandboxed third-party extensions

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | cond | L | could | Owner |

**Spec:** 10 §3.8 (last sentence); 13 §7.2, §13 · **Requirements:** R10, R14 · **Depends on:** SEC-054, UX-050, UX-060

As an owner, I want third-party add-ins to run only inside a boundary that has been reviewed, so that an extension cannot read my token or write past my permissions.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a sandboxed extension, when it runs, then it is in a sandboxed frame without same-origin access, and talks to the editor only through validated messages.
- [x] Given declared read and write levels, when an extension asks for more, then the request is refused.
- [x] Given an extension tries to read the token, storage or the host DOM, when it runs, then it cannot.
- [x] Given an extension writes to the document, when the write is submitted, then it passes the same authority checks as typing.
- [ ] Given the review is complete, when it is recorded, then the exception is listed in the register of SEC-001.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-18; criteria 1 to 4 ticked, criterion 5 not):** Owner decision 120 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md): "Build them". UX-059 and UX-060 are built (criteria 1 to 5 of UX-060 ticked). tests/security/test/sec-055-sandbox-boundary.test.ts (Node.js 22.18, 20 tests, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node tests/security/test/sec-055-sandbox-boundary.test.ts`, passed, repeated five times after a timing fix) is the security lane's own attack on the boundary. Criterion 2: the bridge is driven at write level none, suggest (idle and suggesting) and edit over a recorder of the document API: every write method is refused with `access_denied` and logged as `refused` at none, and at suggest while the editor is not suggesting; 24 method names that are not in the table (`dispose`, `on`, `constructor`, `__proto__`, `toString`, letter case and Unicode look-alikes, a 100,000-character name) never reach the API at any level; a message that carries an `access` member or calls `setAccess` or `access` cannot raise the level; `parseAccess` refuses 14 manifests that declare a level the editor does not offer. Criterion 1: 31 malformed envelopes or arguments get no reply, call nothing and are logged as `ignored`; the real `mountSandboxFrame` over a stand-in document and a real `MessageChannel` sets `sandbox="allow-scripts"` and no `allow` attribute, registers no listener on a window, transfers one port to the frame's window, answers only over that channel, forwards only F6 and a bare Escape, and is closed after dispose; source guards fail if any sandbox source names `allow-same-origin`, `allow-top-navigation`, `allow-popups`, `allow-forms` or any other token, adds a `message` listener, uses `eval`, `new Function` or `innerHTML`, or lets the bridge import anything but the document API. Criterion 3 needs a browser: packages/ui/e2e/ux-060-sandbox-frame.spec.ts, run again in this lane on 2026-11-18 on Windows 11 (`DOCEDIT_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57860 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-060-sandbox-frame.spec.ts --trace=off`, 27 of 27 passed): a hostile extension in the frame tries the host document, a token in `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, a cookie, IndexedDB, the top window and its location, a pop-up and `fetch`, and each is blocked (origin `null`, no request reached the server), the frame cannot navigate itself to carry out what it read, and a forged `postMessage` to the parent is ignored. Criterion 4: packages/element/test/ux-060-sandbox-guards.test.ts (7 tests, passed again in this lane): the bridge in front of a real `DocumentCore`; a write in a region the user may only read, in a locked document and a stale range is refused by the same guards as typing, in suggestion mode it becomes marks, and the same extension is let through in the region for a user who may edit only there. The register row T-3.8-extensions (tests/security/threat-register.json) is now `proven` with the new test, and names the review record.

**Notes:** `cond`: only if sandboxed extensions are built (13 §13 asks whether they are worth their cost). Lane SEC-053-064 (2026-11-13): nothing ticked. The only extension host is tier 1 (`packages/ui/src/extensions/host.ts`, UX-050), which runs in the page with the host's trust (13 §7.2); it has no sandboxed frame, no declared read or write level and no write path for an extension, so criteria 1 to 4 do not hold and no test would prove them. Criterion 5 needs the review itself, which is not done. Lane w8 (2026-11-17), re-checked after the merge of stories/integration: nothing new can be ticked, and nothing is built to work round it. The story depends on UX-060 (the sandboxed frame, not built) and UX-059 (the decision whether to build tier 2 at all; no decision is recorded, the spec default is that tier 2 is not built, and the owner's list of 2026-11-15 does not answer it). `packages/ui/src/extensions` still holds only the tier 1 host (no frame, no declared levels, no write path for an extension). Criteria 1 to 4 would test code that does not exist, and criterion 5 is a review of that code. If UX-059 answers "no", this story goes with UX-060. Lane w8 (2026-11-18): the notes above are out of date. Owner decision 120 answers UX-059 (build tier 2), UX-060 is built, and the story is no longer `cond` in effect (its phase column still says `cond`; the phase is 4 under 11 section 7, which is the main session's edit). Criteria 1 to 4 are ticked on the evidence above. Criterion 5 stays unticked: it reads "given the review is complete", and the review record (docs/review/2026-11-18-ux-060-sandbox-review.md) is a first reading by an agent that says itself it is not the sign-off; the sign-off by a named person is open, and so are its findings 1 to 4 (the host headers `frame-src` and the frame page policy belong in the integration guide, UX-090; no limit on the rate or size of a frame's messages, which the new test shows (a 5 MB string is forwarded); the install step should say that an extension reads what its user may view; the runtime and the frame page are not yet in the element's build). The exception is listed in the register of SEC-001 as a `proven` row, which is the part of criterion 5 that can be done here. What remains needs a human reviewer. Lane w8 (2026-11-26): re-checked. No decision of 2026-11-15 to 2026-11-18 names a reviewer or accepts the findings; the review record (docs/review/2026-11-18-ux-060-sandbox-review.md) still reads "Reviewer (named person): open" and findings 1 to 4 are open and unchanged in code (packages/ui/src/extensions has no budget on frame messages). Criterion 5 stays unticked: it is a sign-off by a named person, which an agent cannot give. Nothing was built to work round it. Observation, not a finding: `parseAccess` reads members inherited from the prototype of the manifest object; the manifest is the host's own object, so the extension cannot reach it.

## Privacy

### SEC-056 Prove users are known only as surrogates and erasure works

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | M | must | Participant |

**Spec:** 10 §4 (first bullet); 08 §13 · **Requirements:** R4, R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, BE-013, HIS-038, HIS-040

As a participant, I want my host identifier and name removable from the service without breaking its records, so that I can be forgotten without altering history.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a test user whose `sub` looks like an e-mail address, when a scenario has run, then the `sub` appears in no table except the mapping row, and in no log, webhook body, export, search entry or audit row.
- [x] Given the user is erased, when the mapping row is deleted, then the display name is gone, the surrogate no longer resolves to anything, and history renders their contributions as an unknown person (owner decision 39; the criterion read "under the surrogate" before).
- [x] Given the log after erasure, when the chain is verified, then it verifies.
- [x] Given another tenant has a user with the same `sub`, when the first is erased, then the second is unaffected.
- [x] Given the integration guide, when it is read, then it asks hosts not to use e-mail addresses as identifiers.

**Evidence:** tests/security/test/sec-056-surrogates-erasure-postgres.test.ts (lane U-SEC-049-064, 2026-11-13, PostgreSQL in the shared container, 3 of 3 pass): criterion 4 and the criterion 2 name and mapping checks through PgUserDirectory, each run in its own database. Criterion 3 (lane U-SEC-049-064): tests/security/test/sec-056-erasure-chain-postgres.test.ts (PostgreSQL in the shared container, Node.js, 1 of 1 passes): a log of four commits authored under the surrogate verifies as intact before and after the erasure, with the same head, and the log holds neither the sub nor the name. tests/security/test/surrogates-erasure.test.ts (lane security-3, 2026-11-13), criterion 4: two tenants with the same `sub` get different surrogates; erasing the sub in one tenant leaves the other tenant's name, mapping and surrogate unchanged (MemoryUserDirectory). Criterion 2 is covered in part: after erasure the display name is null and the surrogate no longer maps to the sub.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-26; criteria 2 and 5 ticked, criterion 1 not):** Owner decision 39 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md): an erased person is shown as "Unknown person" and no per-person surrogate is shown, so criterion 2 is reworded and its history half is met by `shownAuthor` (packages/history/src/erasure.ts). tests/security/test/surrogates-erasure.test.ts (Node.js, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node tests/security/test/surrogates-erasure.test.ts`, 3 of 3 pass): after `erase`, the display name is null, the surrogate no longer resolves, the erased set is derived from the log's surrogates that lead to no sub, and an author run under it (also one with an agent) is shown as `{ kind: 'unknown' }` with no name, sub or surrogate in the label, while another user's run is not. Criterion 5: tests/security/test/sec-056-integration-guide.test.ts (1 of 1 pass) reads apps/reference-host/docs/integration-guide.md, section "The user identifier: `sub`", which says "Not an e-mail address. Do not use e-mail addresses as `sub`", gives the reason (personal data) and says the service stores a surrogate.

**Notes:** Cross-check: moved from phase 1b to 2, because erasure and tombstones (HIS-038, HIS-040) arrive in phase 2; surrogates themselves exist from phase 1a (BE-013). Lane security-3 (2026-11-13): criterion 4 is ticked on the in-memory directory only; the PostgreSQL directory was not run. Lane U-SEC-049-064 (2026-11-13): the PostgreSQL directory now runs in tests/security/test/sec-056-surrogates-erasure-postgres.test.ts (3 of 3 pass), so criterion 4 holds for both stores; criterion 1 still needs a full scenario. Criterion 2 stays open: the history label (packages/history/src/erasure.ts) shows an erased author as an unknown person, not "under the surrogate" as the criterion says. The spec (10 §4, 08 §13) does not settle which is meant, so this is recorded here and not decided. Criteria 1, 3 and 5 stay open: the `sub` scan needs a full scenario, the chain check after erasure is not run here, and the integration guide does not exist yet. Lane U-SEC-049-064 (round 6, 2026-11-13): criterion 1 has its scenario on PostgreSQL in tests/security/test/sec-056-sub-scan-postgres.test.ts (1 of 1 passes): a host sub that looks like an e-mail address mints a token, commits over the WebSocket, reads every content surface, requests an export, searches, changes a grant (the audit row and the dep.grants.changed webhook body), and the service log is kept; then every column of every table of the scenario's database is searched, and the sub is found only in the mapping row tenant_users.sub. It stays unticked: the export file itself is not produced (the harness has no export runner) and the object store is not swept, so the export part of the criterion is not proved. Integration of batch 1 (2026-11-17): the content reads of that scenario no longer include `GET /documents/{id}/attribution` in the corpus, because that route names authors by the host's `sub`, as every user on the wire (decision D42; docs/adr/0373-w5-PRO-055-history-reads.md). Whether the wire may carry a `sub` that is an e-mail address is the owner's question; 10 §4 and 08 §13 say the log and the stores hold surrogates, and say nothing on the wire. Lane w8 (2026-11-17), re-checked after the merge of stories/integration: nothing new can be ticked. Criterion 1 still lacks the export file and the object-store sweep (no export runner in the harness). Criterion 2 waits on the owner's answer on "surrogate or unknown person" for an erased user in history (owner-blockers-2026-11-17.md, Part B; not among decisions 1 to 28). Criterion 5 waits on UX-090 (Integration guide), which is not built: no integration guide exists under docs/. Lane w8 (2026-11-26): criteria 2 and 5 are ticked (see Evidence): decision 39 answers the "surrogate or unknown person" question, and the integration guide now exists in apps/reference-host/docs. Criterion 1 stays unticked: the harness still has no export runner that writes a real file (a stand-in handler would decide what the file holds, so scanning it proves nothing), and the object store is not swept; the scan of the tables, the log, webhook bodies, search and audit answers passes (sec-056-sub-scan-postgres.test.ts). The wire still names authors by `sub` on the attribution and history reads, which the scan leaves out (owner question, unchanged).

### SEC-057 Prove deletion and retention remove what they promise

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | M | must | Tenant administrator |

**Spec:** 10 §4 (third bullet); 08 §11, §13; 12 §7 · **Requirements:** R4, R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, BE-054, BE-052, HIS-029, HIS-030, HIS-038, HIS-040

As a tenant administrator, I want deleting a document to remove its data everywhere the service keeps it, so that I can honour a deletion request.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a document with history, cold segments, checkpoints, assets used nowhere else, exports and search entries, when it is deleted and purged, then a listing of every table and object prefix for its identifier finds nothing, and assets used by other documents remain.
- [x] Given a single commit's payload is replaced by a tombstone, when the chain is verified, then it verifies.
- [x] Given a legal hold on a document, when thinning and purge run, then they do not act on it.
- [x] Given evidence has its own retention period (12 §7), when the document is purged, then evidence follows its own rule and the documentation states it.
- [x] Given per-document encryption keys are built (SEC-062), when a key is destroyed, then the document's data cannot be read from storage or backups.
- [x] Given backups, when a purge has run, then the documentation states when the data ages out of them.

**Evidence:** tests/security/test/deletion-retention.test.ts (Node.js, fast-check, run against packages/history): criterion 2, a tombstoned payload of any commit in a log of 1 to 12 commits still verifies as `intact` with one payload absent, the reader refuses the removed payload and returns the others, and the audit names the actor (100 runs); the checkpoints from the commit's seq on are reported as copies that may hold the text. Criterion 3, in part: while a legal hold is in force, the tombstone is refused for any commit and the thinning plan removes no checkpoint (200 runs).
