**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-26; criteria 1 and 3 to 6 ticked, 6 of 6 with criterion 2):** The document purge now exists (services/document-service/src/control-plane/document-purge.ts, HIS-039). Criterion 1: services/document-service/test/postgres/his-039-purge.pg.test.ts (3 of 3 pass on 2026-11-26 against PostgreSQL in Podman, project docedit-test-w8, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node services/document-service/test/postgres/his-039-purge.pg.test.ts`): a document with commits, grants, a read marker, a job with its export file, a checkpoint row and object, a cold segment, assets and search entries is soft-deleted through the route and purged; the test lists every table of the database by the text of its rows and every object under the document's prefix, and finds only the audit trail and the row of an asset another document uses; the shared asset and its object remain; the search entries of the other document remain. Criterion 3: services/document-service/test/postgres/sec-057-purge-evidence.pg.test.ts (1 of 1 pass, same run): with the retention passed and the document held, the purge skips it (`legal-hold`) and its rows stay; the thinning half is tests/security/test/deletion-retention.test.ts (a hold: no tombstone, no checkpoint removed, 200 runs). Criterion 4 (owner decision 31: signing evidence is kept as long as the signed document exists and deleted with it): the same PostgreSQL test shows evidence rows 400 days old stay while the retention runs and under a hold and are gone after the purge, and only for the purged document; the documentation is docs/operations/retention-and-backups.md, section "Signing evidence", checked by tests/security/test/sec-057-docs-and-keys.test.ts (3 of 3 pass). The sealed final PDF follows its own `retain_until` (his-039 test; frw-043.v-frw-037-054.purge-sealed.pg.test.ts). Criterion 5 (SEC-062 and HIS-041 built the per-document keys): the same file builds the keyed object store over a memory store, destroys the key, and shows that the stored bytes and a backup copy of the object and the key row hold no plaintext, that the read fails with `DocumentKeyDestroyedError`, and that the key service cannot unwrap the backed-up data key; services/document-service/src/storage/document-keys.test.ts is the same check on the service's own parts. The PostgreSQL rows and log reads of a destroyed key are HIS-041's (his-041-room-key.pg.test.ts). Criterion 6: docs/operations/retention-and-backups.md, section "Backups", says the purge acts on the live stores only and that content is gone from backups at the latest at the time of the purge plus the longest period the operator keeps a backup; it gives no figure, since the schedule is the operator's.

**Notes:** The criterion on per-document keys applies only if SEC-062 decides to build them. Criterion 2 is ticked. Criterion 3 stays open: thinning and the tombstone honour the hold (tested), but purge is not in packages/history, so the purge half of "when thinning and purge run" is not proved. Criterion 1 (a listing of every table and object prefix after a purge) needs the document service's purge, which is not built in this tree. Criterion 4 needs evidence retention (12 §7), which the spec does not give a period for. Criterion 5 applies only if SEC-062 builds per-document keys. Criterion 6 needs the backup documentation, which the spec does not yet state in full. Lane w8 (2026-11-26): all six criteria are ticked on the evidence above. The document purge (HIS-039) and the per-document keys (HIS-041) exist now, owner decision 31 answers the evidence period, and decision 30 gives the 30-day retention. One reading is proposed and not decided by the owner: the backup bound in the new document is the purge time plus the operator's longest backup retention (the spec says only that backups age out on their schedule, 08 section 13). The document also states that the key service must not keep a backup of a destroyed key; that is a proposal for SEC-062's custody record. Lane w8 (2026-11-17), re-checked after the merge of stories/integration: nothing new can be ticked. The document service still has no document purge (its `purge` code covers idempotency keys and, in FRW-043, signing evidence only), so criteria 1 and 3 (purge half) cannot be run. Criterion 4 waits on the owner's answer on how long signing evidence is kept (owner-blockers-2026-11-17.md, Part B; not among decisions 1 to 28). Criteria 5 and 6 are as above.

### SEC-058 Prove data stays in the tenant's home region

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | cond | M | could | Tenant administrator |

**Spec:** 10 §4 (second bullet); 06 §7 · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** BE-074, BE-075, SEC-047

As a tenant administrator, I want my data to stay in my home region, so that a residency promise can be kept and shown.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a tenant with a home region, when a scenario has run, then the stores of other regions hold no content, history, evidence or control-plane metadata of that tenant.
- [ ] Given gateways in other regions, when they are inspected, then they persist nothing of the tenant.
- [ ] Given caches of version-pinned content and assets are switched off for the tenant, when users in other regions read, then nothing is cached outside the home region.
- [x] Given logs and metrics leave a region, when they are scanned, then they hold no content (SEC-047).

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-26; criterion 4 ticked):** services/document-service/test/data-plane/sec-058-region-emissions.test.ts (Node.js, 1 of 1 passes, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node services/document-service/test/data-plane/sec-058-region-emissions.test.ts`): two data-plane nodes, `eu` and `us`, a tenant assigned `eu` (BE-074); a client on the `us` gateway types a canary string, the channel and the commit are forwarded to the `eu` node (room and head there, none in `us`); the metrics text and the error reports of both nodes hold no canary. The nodes run in one process over shared in-memory stores, so it shows what each node writes to its metrics and error sink, not a network between regions. SEC-047's tests scan the whole service's logs, metrics and traces (tests/security/test/sec-047-*.test.ts).

**Notes:** `cond`: several regions belong to stage 3. Lane SEC-053-064 (2026-11-13): nothing ticked and nothing built. A search of `services/` for a home region, a regional store or a region-aware gateway finds nothing, and the spec places several regions in stage 3, so criteria 1 to 4 would be tests of code that does not exist. Lane w8 (2026-11-17), re-checked after the merge of stories/integration: nothing new can be ticked. The story depends on BE-074 (home region and forwarding of channels), which has no criterion ticked: no stored home region, no gateway forwarding and no per-region store exist, so the "stores of other regions" and "gateways in other regions" of criteria 1 and 2 have nothing to inspect. Only the version-pinned cache of BE-075 exists (`services/document-service/src/control-plane/pinned-cache.ts`, keyed by tenant, with a per-tenant switch off); it is a single process and not a regional cache, so criterion 3 is not proved by it. Criterion 4 needs the log and metric scan of SEC-047 across regions, and there is only one region. Several regions belong to stage 3 (06 §6); the owner's list of 2026-11-15 does not move them. Lane w8 (2026-11-26): criterion 4 is ticked (see Evidence). Criteria 1 to 3 stay unticked, and nothing is built to work round it. BE-074 now has the home region stored and the gateway forwarding (criteria 1, 2 and 4 of BE-074 ticked), but its own criterion 3 ("stored only in the home region") is open and blocked on the owner's questions 1 and 2 of docs/adr/0403-w5-BE-074-home-region.md (where the assignment lives when each region has its own database; whether a gateway outside the region may read the head and the region from the home region's database) and on BE-068 for the per-region stores (BE-074 Notes, 2026-11-26). The decisions file (224 decisions) has no entry on regions or residency. Criterion 1 ("the stores of other regions hold no content") needs per-region stores to inspect, criterion 2 ("gateways persist nothing") needs the answer to question 2, and criterion 3 needs a read path from another region, which exists only as the single-process pinned cache (BE-075).

## Security testing

### SEC-059 Run fuzzers in CI with a shared corpus

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1a | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 10 §5 (fuzzing) · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-002, FND-001, FND-002

As a maintainer, I want the fuzz targets of 10 §5 registered, run on every change and kept as regression cases, so that parsers and emitters are attacked continuously.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the harness, when it starts, then it registers the targets of 10 §5: the envelope parser and step validation and rebase (phase 1a), the typed emitters per context and language and the math gate (phase 1b), and the rules evaluator and the DOCX importer (phase 2).
- [x] Given each target, when it is defined, then it has a seed corpus drawn from the conformance fixtures and an oracle.
- [x] Given a pull request, when CI runs, then every registered target runs for a bounded time, and a longer run happens nightly.
- [x] Given a crash, a hang, a memory blow-up or an oracle failure, when it is found, then the build fails and the input is saved as a regression case.
- [x] Given the list in 10 §5, when CI runs, then it fails if a listed target that should exist by the current phase is missing.

**Evidence:** Criterion 3 (integrator, batch 5, 2026-11-15): the `check` job of `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `pnpm test` on every pull request, which runs tests/security/test/fuzz.test.ts with its default of 200 generated inputs per target; the new `fuzz-nightly` job of the same file runs on the nightly schedule only, sets `FUZZ_RUNS=5000`, runs `pnpm exec vitest run --project node tests/security/test/fuzz.test.ts` and uploads `tests/security/corpus/fuzz` and `tests/security/corpus/fuzz-inflight` when it fails. The job's command was run by hand on Windows 11 (see the run below and the Notes); the job itself has not run on hosted CI yet. Lane w3 (2026-11-15), criteria 1, 2, 4 and 5: tests/security/test/support/fuzz-harness.ts (the list of 10 §5 with phases, `missingTargets`, `runTarget`), tests/security/test/support/fuzz-targets.ts (seven runnable targets, each calling the real code: `depCodec().decode`, `parseSteps`, `applySteps` and `rebaseOver`, the emitters `text`, `pdfString`, `kvValue` and `url`, `mathGate`, `evaluateExpression`, `importDocx`), tests/security/test/fuzz.test.ts and tests/security/corpus/fuzz (saved regression cases, replayed first). Seeds: every frame and every `steps` array of packages/tck/fixtures/messages; the text and the math of packages/tck/fixtures/canonical/inputs; the expression cases of spec/rules/rules-1.fixtures.json; the three smallest files of each folder of packages/export-docx/corpus. The harness is tested on targets made to fail: a crash, an oracle failure, an input over the time limit and an input that grows the heap past the limit each fail the run and save the shrunk input, which alone fails the next run; an input left in flight by a killed run is saved by the next run, which fails; a registry without a due target, or with a name that has no seeds or oracle, is reported. Run: `pnpm exec vitest run --project node tests/security/test/fuzz.test.ts`, Node.js on Windows 11, 2026-11-15, 18 of 18 passed with 200 generated inputs per target (25 for the importer) in about 9 s, and again with `FUZZ_RUNS=5000` in about 78 s; no finding in either run. Earlier evidence, superseded by the audit of 2026-11-13: test/fuzz.test.ts registers the phase-1a targets (envelope parser, step validation, rebase) with seed corpora and oracles, lists the 1b and phase-2 targets, runs each bounded on a pull request (FUZZ_RUNS raises it nightly), replays known regressions and fails if a phase-1a target is missing: decode never throws and always classifies to a known verdict; parseSteps fails only with a registry code; apply and rebase never throw and an accepted apply yields a document that still checks.

**Notes:** Integrator, batch 5 (2026-11-15): the nightly job the lane asked for was added to ci.yml as `fuzz-nightly`, and criterion 3 is ticked on the workflow as written; it has not run on hosted CI, so untick it if its first scheduled run fails. `FUZZ_RUNS=5000` is a proposed value (the run the lane measured), for the owner to set with the other limits. The lane's note, written before that job existed, follows. Lane w3 (2026-11-15): the audit's five points were answered by a rebuilt harness (Evidence). Criterion 3 stays unticked: a pull request runs every target bounded, because the `test` job of `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `pnpm test`, which includes this file; but no scheduled job sets `FUZZ_RUNS`, so there is no longer nightly run, and `.github/` is root configuration that this lane may not edit. Needed from the main session: in the nightly part of ci.yml, a step that runs `pnpm exec vitest run --project node tests/security/test/fuzz.test.ts` with `FUZZ_RUNS` raised, and uploads `tests/security/corpus/fuzz` and `tests/security/corpus/fuzz-inflight` as artifacts when it fails. Limits of what is ticked, none decided here: (a) the time limit (1,000 ms per input), the heap limit (256 MiB of growth per input) and the run counts are proposed values, not figures from the spec; the owner sets them (ADR 0121 item 39). (b) The importer's seeds come from the DOCX import corpus, because no conformance fixture holds a DOCX file. The math gate has two seed formulas only, all that the canonical documents hold. (c) A hang that never returns cannot be stopped in process: its input is in the in-flight file and becomes a regression case on the next run in the same workspace, so on hosted CI it is kept only if the job uploads that folder. (d) Memory is checked as heap growth over one input; a process killed for memory is caught the same way as a hang. (e) The current phase is `FUZZ_PHASE`, default `1a`; nothing machine-readable in the repository names the phase, so it is moved by hand at each phase end. All seven targets are registered already, so the check passes for every phase. Earlier notes follow. The spec sets no duration, corpus size or coverage goal (see Open questions). Phase inferred: the envelope parser and step validation exist in 1a. Audit 2026-11-13: all five ticks removed. tests/security/test/fuzz.test.ts passes and fuzzes real code (depCodec, parseSteps, applySteps, rebaseOver), but its registry is a constant list of ids that nothing reads, so only the three phase-1a targets are runnable and the other four are names (criterion 1); the seeds are four hand-written strings for the envelope parser and none for step validation or rebase, none drawn from conformance fixtures (criterion 2); no CI job sets FUZZ_RUNS, so there is no longer nightly run (criterion 3); a failing input is not saved, the REGRESSIONS list is hand-written and only slow inputs are stored by fuzz-time-limit.test.ts, and memory blow-up is not checked (criterion 4); the phase check asserts the length of that constant list, so a missing target cannot fail it (criterion 5).

### SEC-060 Check that every authorisation row and rejection code has a negative test

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 10 §5 (negative conformance tests); 07 §12; 05 §5.8, §11 · **Requirements:** R3, R6, R10 · **Depends on:** PRO-115, PRM-056

As a maintainer, I want the matrix and the error registry to be covered by negative tests, checked by the build, so that a new operation or code cannot ship without one.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the authorisation matrix, when it is stored in a machine-readable file, then each row has an identifier.
- [x] Given the error registry, when it is stored in a machine-readable file, then each code has an identifier, its surfaces, its retryability and its client action.
- [x] Given the test suites, when tests are tagged with a row or a code, then CI fails if a row has no negative test (a caller one capability short is refused) or a code has no test that provokes it and checks its `code`, `action` and `retryable`.
- [x] Given a code that cannot be provoked from outside, when it is listed, then it is marked with the reason and tested by the fault hooks (BE-079) or by a unit test.
- [x] Given codes that belong to a later profile (`field_invalid`, `conflict`, `workflow_active`), when their profile lands, then the check includes them.

**Evidence:** test/catalogue-rows.test.ts: spec/permissions/permissions-1.json matrix rows each have a unique identifier and number, an operation and a requirement (criterion 1); spec/dep/dep-1.registry.json error codes each have a unique code, at least one surface, a retryability and a client action (criterion 2). packages/tck/test/sec-060-negative-coverage.test.ts (lane w8, Node.js, 7 tests pass), criteria 3 to 5: the gate reads the 13 rows of the matrix and the registry's codes and fails for a row with no negative test, a code that no scenario provokes and has no reason, a code the kit cannot provoke that has no test tagged `[neg:code <code>]`, a tag that names a row or code that does not exist, and a code of a landed profile that the kit does not provoke; each failure is shown on a mutated copy of the evidence. The tags are in titles of existing tests: tests/security/test/permission-matrix.test.ts (asset.upload), services/document-service/test/control-plane/prm-057-row7-asset-read.test.ts (asset.read), and, for codes, packages/authority/test/room.test.ts (busy), services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-025.workflow-resource.test.ts (workflow_active), services/document-service/test/data-plane/session-1b.test.ts (rate_limited, slow_consumer), services/document-service/src/control-plane/be-091-live-quotas.test.ts (quota_exceeded), services/document-service/src/data-plane/hooks.test.ts (unavailable), services/document-service/test/server/server.test.ts (internal).

**Notes:** Phase inferred: the conformance kit's negative tests start with the `core` scenarios of 1b. Lane w8 (2026-11-17): the check is `packages/tck/test/sec-060-negative-coverage.test.ts` and runs under `pnpm test`. Evidence for a row is a scenario of the kit's `authz` profile that declares what it exercises (each refuses a caller without the capability with `forbidden` and allows one with it) or a test titled `[neg:row <id>]`; evidence for a code is a scenario that declares it in `covers.errors`, and the registry profile checks the code, surface, retry hint and `sync.reject` action of each such provocation against the registry. Criterion 4: the codes the kit cannot provoke from outside carry the reason in `PROVOKED` (`packages/tck/src/profiles/registry.ts`) and are tested by an in-process unit or fault-hook test tagged in the title; those tests check the `code`, and some check `action` or `retryable` too, but the check does not require it (BE-079's fault hooks are not all built, so `unavailable` and `internal` use the service's own hooks and worker tests). Criterion 5: `conflict` and `field_invalid` belong to the `fields` profile, which has scenarios and provokes both, so they are held to the kit; `workflow_active` has no workflow profile yet and is accepted through its tagged unit test, and the check refuses that fallback as soon as a `workflow` profile has scenarios (shown on a mutated copy). How many matrix rows any test names has not been measured.

## Open items of 10 §6

### SEC-061 Decide the abuse-handling policy for hosted mode

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| decision | cond | S | should | Operator |

**Spec:** 10 §6 (second item); 06 §16 · **Requirements:** R10 · **Depends on:** BE-097

As an operator of the hosted service, I want a policy for malware distributed through assets and phishing documents, so that abuse reports have a defined handling and a defined limit.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a report, when the policy is written, then it says how reports arrive, who decides, and what can be done to a document, an asset or a tenant (lock, archive, delete, suspend).
- [x] Given content is involved, when the policy is written, then it says what the operator may read and refers to the structural dump of BE-089 as the default way to look.
- [x] Given the policy and the legal-hold rule, when both apply, then the policy says which wins.
- [ ] Given the policy needs tooling, when it is accepted, then the tools become stories.

**Evidence:** docs/adr/0156-security-3-backlog-batch.md (section SEC-061): a proposed abuse policy covering intake, who decides, the five actions in increasing severity, what the operator may read (the BE-089 structural dump by default) and the rule that a legal hold wins over deletion. Proposed, not accepted by the owner.

**Notes:** `cond`: only if the owner chooses hosted mode (11 §10, decision 8). The default taken: both modes designed for, neither scheduled first. Criteria 1 to 3 are ticked on the proposed policy; criterion 4 applies on acceptance. Lane w8 (2026-11-17): the owner's answer 28 of 2026-11-15 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md; both hosted and self-hosted from the start) removes the condition that held this story back, so the policy now applies and ADR 0156 (section SEC-061) is reworded to say so; the proposal itself is unchanged. Criterion 4 stays unticked: the answer does not accept the policy. Still the owner's: the second approver, and that a legal hold wins over deletion while an abuse lock stays (ADR 0156, open question 4). The tools the policy needs are listed in the ADR as proposed stories (intake channel, asset quarantine flag, document lock and archive flags, tenant suspension control, audit of operator reads of content). They become stories when the owner accepts the policy; none is added to the backlog here. Lane w8 (2026-11-26), nothing new ticked: owner answer 97 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md) says agents draft the policy and controls and nothing is final until the owner and counsel have reviewed it, so criterion 4 stays unticked until that review.

Lane w3 (2026-11-17), criterion 3: docs/adr/0156-security-3-backlog-batch.md (section SEC-062, "Sealing party") now follows decision 14 of docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md (the operator seals by default; a tenant's own key and certificate where the tenant supplies them) and lists what changes for the signing service (per-tenant key resolution with the operator's key as default, the key named in the seal's evidence, tenant keys used only through the signing service, key loading as a tenant administration action). ### SEC-063 Decide whether to anchor chain heads in a public transparency log

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| decision | 4 | S | could | Relying party |

**Spec:** 10 §6 (fourth item); 08 §12 · **Requirements:** R4, R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-029

As a relying party, I want to know whether history can be checked against something outside the operator and the host, so that tamper evidence does not rest on one party.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the existing anchors (the host through webhooks and manifests, write-once storage), when the decision is recorded, then it compares them with a public transparency log.
- [x] Given a public log, when the decision is recorded, then it states what would be published (a hash only), and the privacy effect.
- [x] Given the decision is yes, when it is accepted, then the stories to build it are added.

**Evidence:** docs/adr/0156-security-3-backlog-batch.md (section SEC-063): compares the webhook and manifest heads and the write-once store with a public log, and gives the options with what each publishes and its privacy effect (option C publishes only a salted root). Proposed, not accepted by the owner.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-18, criterion 3):** docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md decision 143 (yes, anchor chain heads in a public log; the choice of log goes in an ADR). docs/adr/0409-w8-SEC-063-public-transparency-log.md records what is published (a salted periodic root per tenant, option C), who holds the salt, the port, the failure behaviour and the proposed log. The stories to build it are SEC-065, SEC-066, SEC-067 and SEC-068 below. docs/spec/10-security.md (§3.3 and §6) and docs/spec/08-history.md (§12) are changed to match.

**Notes:** Decision 4 of 10 §6. The default taken, pending the owner: no public log in version 1 (option A). Criteria 1 and 2 are ticked on the proposed record. Criterion 3 stays open because it applies only if the owner says yes. Lane w8 (2026-11-17), re-checked after the merge of stories/integration: no change. The owner's decisions of 2026-11-15 and 2026-11-17 do not cover a public transparency log, so the default (option A, no public log in version 1) stands as proposed and no stories are added. Lane w8 (2026-11-18): that note was out of date. Decision 143 of docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md answers yes and reverses the default, so criterion 3 applies and is ticked. ADR 0409 records the form (option C, a salted periodic root, never a hash per document) and proposes the log (a tile-based, witness-cosigned log, first target the Sigstore public-good Rekor log). The owner asked for the choice to go in an ADR; the log named there is a proposal that the owner accepts or changes. SEC-065 to SEC-068 are added. Criteria 1 and 2 stand, with option C as the chosen form.

### SEC-065 Build the periodic salted root over a tenant's chain heads

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 4 | M | could | Relying party |

**Spec:** 10 §3.3, §6; 08 §12; ADR 0409 · **Requirements:** R4, R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-063, SEC-029, BE-081

As a relying party, I want each tenant's chain heads folded into one root that reveals nothing about any document, so that one public entry can vouch for all of them.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a tenant with documents whose chains have heads, when a period ends, then the service builds a Merkle tree whose leaves are the hash of the head, its sequence number and the tenant's salt, and records the root, the period and the leaf count.
- [x] Given a document that did not change in the period, when the tree is built, then its latest head is still a leaf, so a later check of any head finds a root that covers it.
- [x] Given a leaf, when an inclusion proof is asked for with the salt, then the proof verifies against the root; given a wrong salt, a changed head or another sequence number, then it does not.
- [x] Given the same inputs, when the tree is built twice, then the root is the same (a property test over random heads and orderings).
- [x] Given the salt, when it is created, stored and rotated, then it is held under the tenant's key rules (SEC-062) and is never written to a log, a webhook or the public entry.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-26):** Pure rules in `packages/history/src/public-root.ts` (leaf `SHA-256(0x02 ‖ salt ‖ seq ‖ head)`, leaves sorted by hash, the node rule of head-anchor.ts, proof, verification, the public entry text with no identifier members), tested in `packages/history/test/public-root.test.ts` (7 tests, with fast-check properties: same root in any order and over repeated builds, every head's proof verifies, a wrong salt, a changed head and another sequence number do not verify and cannot be proved; criteria 3 and 4). The service in `services/document-service/src/control-plane/public-root.ts` (`PublicRoots.run`, `PublicRoots.proof`, `TenantSalts`), tested in `services/document-service/src/control-plane/public-root.test.ts` (10 tests, memory stores and `MemoryKeyService`): a run records the root, the period (`from`, `to`) and the leaf count (criterion 1); a document that did not change keeps its latest head as a leaf and its proof verifies against the later root (criterion 2); the salt is 32 random bytes wrapped with the tenant's `unwrap` key (`tenant-salt-<tenant>`), stored only wrapped, rotated by version with old versions kept, unrecoverable after the key is destroyed, and its hex and base64 forms appear in no file, audit event, stored record or public entry (criterion 5). Run with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on both files; type-check of both packages clean.

**Notes:** The salt is held under the tenant's key rules as `KeyService` wrap and unwrap (SEC-062, accepted by decision 205); the two-person recovery rule is the key service's own procedure and is not built here. The heads source (`PublicRootHeads`), the salt rows and the root rows are interfaces with memory implementations; the PostgreSQL implementations and the scheduled task that calls `PublicRoots.run` once per period are wired in SEC-066, which also fixes the period default, so this story builds and tests the rules and the service logic only. `node` and `levelsOf` in `head-anchor.ts` are now exported (additive) so that the two trees share one node rule.

### SEC-066 Submit roots to a public transparency log and keep the receipts

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 4 | M | could | Operator |

**Spec:** 10 §6; 08 §12; ADR 0409 · **Requirements:** R4, R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-065, BE-081

As an operator, I want each root sent to a public log through a port, so that the log can be changed or turned off without touching the commit path.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a root, when it is submitted through the `TransparencyLog` port, then the service stores the receipt (log identifier, entry index, signed checkpoint) with the signed heads in write-once storage.
- [x] Given the log is not reachable, when a submission fails, then it is retried, the root stays in the write-once store, and no commit, open or export waits for it.
- [x] Given a local log of the same format as the proposed public log, when an adapter submits to it, then the receipt's checkpoint signature verifies against the log's key. The public log itself is a proposal in ADR 0409 and is called only after the owner accepts it.
- [x] Given a self-hosted operator, when the port is pointed at another log or turned off, then the service works, and the verification report shows that no public anchor exists for the tenant.
- [ ] Given the default period, when it is chosen, then it comes from a measurement of the log's limits and the wanted detection delay, and the measurement says how and where it was taken.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-26):** The port and receipt are in `services/document-service/src/transparency/log-port.ts` (`TransparencyLog`, `LogReceipt` with log identifier, entry index, inclusion proof and signed checkpoint, `checkReceipt`); the log format parts (RFC 6962 tree and inclusion proofs, C2SP signed note with Ed25519, checkpoint body, verifier key text) in `transparency/tlog.ts`, tested in `transparency/tlog.test.ts` (every leaf of every tree size 1 to 40 proves and nothing else does; a note opens only with its own key and text). A local log of that format, served over HTTP on an ephemeral port, and the adapter for it are in `transparency/local-log.ts`. The submission is `services/document-service/src/control-plane/public-root-submit.ts` (`PublicRootAnchors.submitPending`, `report`, `publicRootSubmitTask`), tested in `control-plane/public-root-submit.test.ts` (7 tests, run with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on the three files: 23 tests passed; type-check and eslint clean). Criterion 1: after a run the receipt is written under `anchors/public/<period>.receipt.json` next to the signed heads through the write-once file port (`MemoryHeadAnchorFiles` in the test; the S3 Object Lock implementation of the same port is the one the hourly anchors use) and kept with the root. Criterion 2: with the log at a dead address the root file and the root row stay, the run reports the failure and changes nothing that says "anchored", a later run submits and gets the same index; a log that never answers is given up after the timeout; nothing on the commit, open or export path calls the submitter. Criterion 3: the receipt's checkpoint signature verifies against the local log's key and fails against another key, a changed entry and a changed proof (`checkReceipt`). Criterion 4: with the port off (`log: null`) roots are built and nothing is sent, and `report` says "No public anchor exists for this tenant: the transparency log is turned off".

**Notes:** Criterion 5 stays unticked. The default period must come from a measurement of the public log's limits and the wanted detection delay, and the public log is only a proposal in ADR 0409 (decision 143 asks for an ADR; the owner has not accepted Rekor or another log). A measurement against the local log would say nothing about the public log's limits, so none is made here and no default is chosen: `publicRootBuildTask` and `publicRootSubmitTask` take the period as an argument, as ADR 0409 decision 3 says. The adapter is built against the port; the public log is not called. The local log implements the checkpoint and proof formats of the proposed log (signed note, RFC 6962 hashing) but not its tile read API or witness cosigning; the adapter says only what it checks (the log's own checkpoint signature). Not wired here: the tasks are not added to `server.ts`, and the PostgreSQL rows for roots, salts and receipts are not written (memory stores behind the same interfaces); both need root-level composition that this lane leaves to the integrator. `objectKeys` gained `publicRoot`, `publicRootHeads` and `publicRootReceipt` (additive).

### SEC-067 Let a relying party check history against the public log

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 4 | M | could | Relying party |

**Spec:** 10 §6; 08 §12; ADR 0409 · **Requirements:** R4, R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-066, BE-081

As a relying party, I want to take a chain head and check it against the public log without asking the operator, so that history can be trusted without trusting the operator or the host.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a head, its salt and an inclusion proof, when the verification command (BE-081) runs with the log's public key, then it checks the proof, the receipt's checkpoint and the chain up to the head, and reports each result.
- [x] Given a chain rewritten after its root was published, when the command runs, then it reports that the rewritten head is not in the published root.
- [x] Given a head committed after the last published root, when the command runs, then it reports "not yet anchored" with the period's end, not a failure; the documentation states this window.
- [x] Given no network, when the command runs with a saved receipt, then it verifies offline.
- [x] Given an audit export, when it is built, then it carries the receipts and proofs it needs.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-26):** The check is `services/document-service/src/transparency/verify-public-anchor.ts` (`verifyPublicAnchor`) and the command `services/document-service/scripts/verify-public-anchor.ts` (input: public anchor evidence or an audit segment, `--log-key`, optional `--bundle`; output: one JSON report; exit 0 verified, 1 failed, 2 incomplete, 3 unreadable, 4 not yet anchored). Tests in `services/document-service/src/transparency/verify-public-anchor.test.ts` (9 tests) and `services/document-service/test/control-plane/sec-067-audit-export.test.ts` (3 tests), run with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node`; type-check and eslint clean; the existing `his-037-audit-segment.test.ts` and the history tests still pass (40 files, 346 tests). Criterion 1: the report gives the entry, the proof of the head in the root (salt and path), the receipt (leaf, checkpoint signature with the log's key, checkpoint, inclusion proof) and the chain with the anchored head's match, each on its own. Criterion 2: a consistent chain rewritten from seq 3 gives status failed with "the head of the log at seq 5 is not in the published root" (library and command, exit 1). Criterion 3: a log that reaches past the anchored seq gives "not yet anchored" with `afterSeq`, `logHead` and `periodEnd` (library; command exit 4); the window is stated in docs/spec/10-security.md §6. Criterion 4: the command runs as a child process on a saved evidence file, key file and bundle with the local log already stopped, and verifies (exit 0); the verifier makes no network call. Criterion 5: `GET /documents/{id}/audit-segment` carries `publicAnchors` (the entry, the receipt, the salt, the head and the path of the newest root that covers a head inside the range) through `HandlerDeps.publicAnchors`, and the segment verifies offline with the log's key; a document with no root carries none.

**Notes:** Proposed, for the owner to confirm: the salt travels in the audit export to every caller who may read the document's history (`read.history`), because the proof cannot be checked without it and that caller already holds the document's chain heads; the owner may restrict it to tenant administrators. The command is its own script beside the BE-081 recovery tool and not a subcommand of `recovery-cli.ts`, because that tool needs a database and an object store and this check must not. `PublicAnchorExport` is built and tested in the memory composition of the service (`parts.publicAnchors`); `server.ts` does not create it yet, because the PostgreSQL rows for roots, salts and receipts are not written (see SEC-066 notes), so a running service carries no public anchor until that composition is added. Cosignatures of witnesses are not checked (the format of the proposed log allows them; the adapter and the checker use the log's own signature only). Additive edits outside this story's files: `HandlerDeps` in `control-plane/kit.ts`, `ServiceParts` in `server.ts` and `control-plane/audit-segment-route.ts` (one optional member and its use), and `AuditSegment.publicAnchors` in `@docedit/history`.

### SEC-068 Prove the public log holds nothing but a root

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 4 | S | could | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 10 §4, §6; ADR 0409, ADR 0156 · **Requirements:** R4, R10 · **Depends on:** SEC-065, SEC-066

As a maintainer, I want a test that shows what leaves the system, so that a change cannot start publishing identifiers.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a tenant with canary document, tenant and user identifiers and canary text, when a root is submitted, then the bytes sent to the log hold none of them and hold no sequence number.
- [x] Given two documents with the same head hash in two tenants, when roots are built, then the leaves differ because of the salts.
- [x] Given a tenant's salt is destroyed, when a head is checked, then the proof cannot be built, and the public entry stays and links to nothing.
- [x] Given a code change that adds a field to the submission, when the test runs, then it fails until the field is listed with a reason.

**Evidence (lane w8, 2026-11-26):** `services/document-service/test/transparency/sec-068-public-log-privacy.test.ts` (5 tests, run with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node`) and, in `@docedit/history`, `PUBLIC_ROOT_FIELD_REASONS` and `unlistedPublicEntryFields` (`packages/history/src/public-root.ts`). Criterion 1: every request the adapter makes to a local log (method, URL, headers, body, as text and hexadecimal) is recorded and searched for a canary tenant, document and user identifier, canary text, two canary sequence numbers and the chain heads: none is there; the one POST body is exactly the public entry, which has no `seq` member. Criterion 2: the same head in two tenants gives different leaves and roots, and one tenant's salt does not cover the other's root. Criterion 3: after the tenant's salt key is destroyed `PublicRoots.proof` rejects, a new salt cannot be made, the entry stays in the log and in write-once storage, and no guessed salt covers the head; the entry holds no head. Criterion 4: the entry's members must equal `PUBLIC_ROOT_MEMBERS`, each must have a reason in `PUBLIC_ROOT_FIELD_REASONS` (a type error if a member has none), and the test shows `unlistedPublicEntryFields` naming a `tenant` member added to an entry (which `parsePublicEntry` also refuses).

**Notes:** The test searches what the adapter sends over HTTP to the local log; it does not and cannot test what the proposed public log (Rekor) would be sent, which is the same entry through the same port. The search covers the strings and the sequence numbers of the canaries; a leak in another encoding would not be found. Published by design, and listed with reasons: the period's end and the number of leaves (ADR 0409 decision 1).

## Read restrictions (ADR 0019)

Added on 2026-11-06 from spike S12 (SPK-029; `docs/spikes/2026-11-06-s12-read-restrictions.md`), after the owner decided that version 1 can hide regions and field values from some readers (SPK-005). Every other story that serves content, steps, positions, history, search, exports, comments, presence or field values applies the requester's projection: README §2.2 rule 8.

### SEC-064 Prove hidden content never reaches a reader without access

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | L | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** ADR 0019; 10 · **Requirements:** R3, R10 · **Depends on:** SYN-051, BE-098, EXP-123

As a maintainer, I want an end-to-end leak test across every surface, so that a missed path is found before release.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given canary strings in a confidential region's text and title and in a sensitive value, when a reader without access uses every surface (socket messages, REST reads, assets, exports, search, history, presence, comments and logs sent to clients), then no canary appears in anything they receive.
- [x] Given grant changes, lost notifications and failovers during the run, then the same holds, and the window of stale reads is measured and reported.
- [x] Given a new endpoint or message type added without a projection test, when the check runs, then it fails, as SEC-060 does for authorisation rows.

**Evidence:** tests/security/test/read-restrictions.test.ts and sec-064-hidden-surfaces.test.ts (earlier lanes): socket frames and every REST operation, the log. tests/security/test/sec-064-w8-surfaces.test.ts (lane w8, Node.js, memory and PostgreSQL, 12 of 12 pass), criterion 1: content, field values, thread list and attribution at every sequence number, the commits, history, diff, activity, versions and grants with the query each route needs; presence and thread notices on an open channel (a granted reader sees the commit, the thread and the user inside the region, so the checks can fail); the real export worker handler builds the `cdm`, `html` and `tex` exports of the requester's projection, and the owner's export holds the text where the reader's does not; an image placed only inside the hidden region resolves to a URL for a granted reader and not for one without access; search; the log. tests/security/test/sec-064-w8-faults.test.ts (lane w8, 4 of 4 pass; the PostgreSQL cases use the shared PostgreSQL and S3-compatible containers), criterion 2: six grant and revoke cycles with a reader who never had access reading throughout, a canary commit after each revocation that the revoked channel does not receive, a second service node that is not told of a change made through the first, and a restart of the service on the same database and port. tests/security/test/read-projection-coverage.test.ts, criterion 3: a message type or a read endpoint without an entry fails, and neither a new gap nor a new swept-only endpoint is allowed (shown on a mutated list).

**Notes:** Lane security-3 (2026-11-13). `tests/security/test/read-restrictions.test.ts` runs the service with the production read restriction (`visibility`; the harness takes a `views` option for it) over a document with a confidential region (canary title and text) and a sensitive field value. A reader with no grant calls every operation of `DEP1.operations` and gets no canary, before and after a commit into the region; the reader's channel gets no canary and no steps of that commit, and a write it attempts is refused with `sync.reject` carrying no canary; the service log holds none. A reader with access sees the canaries, so the check can fail. Revocation with an open channel: no stale read after the change; the first clean read came 6 ms, 10 ms and 17 ms after the change's request in three in-process runs (memory storage, wall clock). The open channel was not told of the change within 1 s (no `perm.changed`, no close): an open finding for the owner. `tests/security/test/read-projection-coverage.test.ts` requires every server message type of `DEP1.messages` to carry an entry: exercised, no content (with reason), or gap (with reason). Eleven types are gaps and pass the gate. PostgreSQL cases were skipped: PostgreSQL is not running here, so nothing is claimed for it. Lane U-SEC-049-064 (2026-11-13): the PostgreSQL cases now run in the shared test container (`docedit-test-postgres`, `pnpm vitest run tests/security/test/read-restrictions.test.ts`): 12 of 12 pass. The first run found two 500 responses for a bare identifier that is not a UUID (`completeAsset` with `missing-aid`, `getExport` with `missing-jid`): PostgreSQL rejected the uuid cast. Fixed in `services/document-service/src/control-plane/assets.ts` (`get` and `many`) and `services/document-service/src/control-plane/jobs/pg.ts` (`get`), which now answer no such asset or job; `be-042-043-assets.test.ts`, `routes.assets.test.ts`, `routes.exports.test.ts` and `priority.test.ts` still pass in PostgreSQL. Stabilisation group 5, round 1 fix (2026-11-13): `getThread` and `deleteThread` answered 500 on PostgreSQL for a thread identifier that is not a UUID; `PgThreadStore.get` and `delete` (`services/document-service/src/storage/thread-store.ts`) now answer no such thread without a query, as the memory store does, so the route gives 404. `read-restrictions.test.ts` passes 12 of 12 on both storages and `pro-059-threads.test.ts` still passes. Criteria 1 to 3 stay open as above; the PostgreSQL run adds no canary surface. Criterion 1 stays open: assets, exports, comments and presence hold no canary data in these runs (their routes are swept with absent identifiers only). Criterion 2 stays open: lost notifications and failovers are not run, and the notification gap above is unresolved. Criterion 3 stays open for the same reason as SEC-060: gaps are recorded exceptions and pass the gate. Open question for the owner, recorded in `docs/adr/0156-security-3-backlog-batch.md`: an edit inside a hidden region reaches a reader without access as a commit envelope (sequence, author and time, no steps). Nothing was changed to decide it. Lane U-SEC-049-064 (round 7, 2026-11-13): `tests/security/test/sec-064-hidden-surfaces.test.ts` seeds a thread on the confidential region (its comment carries a canary), an owner's asset (its name carries a canary) and an export job, then sweeps every DEP1 operation as the reader without access with those identifiers; it passes 12 of 12 (`pnpm vitest run tests/security/test/sec-064-hidden-surfaces.test.ts`, memory and PostgreSQL in the shared container docedit-test-postgres). Found: `GET /documents/{id}/threads` (DEP1 `listThreads`) is not offered by the service (404); no REST read returns a comment body (`GET /threads/{tid}` returns state, assignee and target), so the comment's canary is stored but never served, and that surface is checked for absence only. Criterion 1 stays open: the export file is not produced (no export runner), presence carries no seeded canary, and the asset is not attached to a hidden region. Criteria 2 and 3 are unchanged. Lane w8 (2026-11-17), all three ticked. Criterion 1: the earlier sweep called history, commits, diff, attribution and field values without the query they require, so they mostly answered 400; the new sweep fills the query and runs it at every sequence number, after a commit into the region. Presence is relayed within a view class (`presence.ts`), so a reader outside the class never receives a user inside the region; the test grants a second user the region and checks that the granted reader sees that user's identifier and the outsider's channel does not. No REST route serves a comment body (`GET /threads/{tid}` returns state and target), so a comment's canary is checked on the notices only, and the thread notice reaches the granted reader and not the outsider. Limits: exports are built as `cdm`, `html` and `tex` (the compile input of the PDF), because no TeX Live image is on this machine, so a PDF file is not scanned; the search index is fed by the test, because the checkpoint hook that feeds it (BE-054) is not built, and the host search returns identifiers and scores only. Criterion 2, measured on 2026-11-17 on this machine, one process, wall-clock, six cycles each: from the request of a revocation to the first answer without the text, median 21 to 33 ms and maximum 745 to 845 ms in memory, median 78 to 84 ms and maximum 754 to 811 ms on PostgreSQL (the time includes the revocation request itself); reads that still carried the text after the revocation was acknowledged: 0 of every cycle. The earlier open finding is closed: the revoked channel now receives `ch.closed` (6 of 6 cycles in each storage, median 36 to 54 ms in memory and 115 to 129 ms on PostgreSQL; no `perm.changed`). Through a second node that was not told of a change made through the first, the first answer without the text came after 108 to 148 ms, 0 stale reads (one PostgreSQL database, two processes). After a restart on the same database and port, the first answer without the text came after 967 to 1760 ms, 0 stale reads. The windows are measurements of these runs, not limits. Not tested: a lost message between two nodes through a network fault (no fault hook injects one here; BE-079), and a failover where the new node starts while the old one still runs. Criterion 3: the gate now also lists every read endpoint a user token may call; the message gaps that remain (`error`, `chunk`, `sync.caughtUp`, `sync.reset`, `sync.check`, `perm.changed`, `wf.changed`) are a fixed list that cannot grow, and `getDocument`, `getImport`, `getFonts` and the workflow reads are swept only with absent identifiers (a fixed list). Open question still for the owner (ADR 0156): a commit made in a hidden region reaches the reader without access as an envelope (sequence, author, time, no steps).

## Coverage

| Section of 10 | Covered by |
|---|---|
| §1 What is protected | none: informative (the assets are the register's headings, SEC-001) |
| §2 Actors and trust | SEC-001, SEC-002 |
| §3.1 Editor component | SEC-005, SEC-006, SEC-007, FE-125, SEC-009, SEC-010, SEC-036 (hostile PDF); open shadow root: none: informative (SEC-009 documents it) |
| §3.2 Protocol and gateway | SEC-012 to SEC-020 |
| §3.3 Authority | SEC-021 to SEC-032 |
| §3.4 LaTeX workers | SEC-033, SEC-034, SEC-035, SEC-036, SEC-042 |
| §3.5 Asset and import workers | SEC-037, SEC-038, SEC-039, SEC-040 |
| §3.6 Signing and evidence | SEC-041, SEC-042, SEC-043, SEC-044, SEC-022 (a filler changing more than their fields) |
| §3.7 Data and tenancy | SEC-045, SEC-046, SEC-047, SEC-048, SEC-049 |
| §3.8 Supply chain | SEC-050, SEC-051, SEC-052, SEC-053, SEC-054, SEC-055 |
| §4 Privacy | SEC-056 (surrogates and erasure), SEC-058 (home region), SEC-057 (deletion and retention); end-to-end encryption: none: non-goal |
| §5 Security testing | Fuzzing SEC-059; property tests SEC-021, SEC-022, SEC-023; negative conformance tests SEC-060; adversarial sandbox tests SEC-035; review SEC-003 and SEC-004 |
| §6 Open issues | SEC-011 (browser extensions), SEC-061 (abuse handling), SEC-062 (key management), SEC-063 (transparency log), SEC-065 to SEC-068 (build it) |
